cannot both belong to
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
since otherwise we would have
\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{P}_{3}
, thus contradicting (
3.46
). Therefore, we can assume that
j_{i,1}\notin\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
. We now distinguish two cases:

•

If
j_{i,2}\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
, then it cannot belong to
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
(since it already has a sibling in
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
) so that
K(j_{i,2})(\kappa)=k_{j_{i,2}}
.

•

If
j_{i,2}\notin\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
, then for
q=1,2
and by definition of
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
,
K(j_{i,q})
is a signed combination of
\left((K(j))_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)},(K(j_{i^{\prime}}))_{i\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}\right)
(we used that
j_{i^{\prime}}>_{\tilde{\rho}}j_{i,q}
implies
i^{\prime}\geq i
to exclude the lower element of
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
). More precisely, there exists numbers
s_{q}\in\{-1,0,1\}
for
q=1,2
such that

\displaystyle K(j_{i,q})-s_{q}K(j_{i})\in\mathrm{Vect}\left((K(j))_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)},(K(j_{i^{\prime}}))_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}\right).

(3.63)

The relation
K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))=K(j_{i})+K(j_{i,1})+K(j_{i,2})
and the fact that
K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))\in\mathrm{Vect}\left((K(j))_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)},(K(j_{i^{\prime}}))_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle(1+s_{1}+s_{2})K(j_{i})\in\mathrm{Vect}\left((K(j))_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\{j_{i}\}}\right).

The linear independence of
(K(j))_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)}
implies
1+s_{1}+s_{2}=0
, so that there must exist
q_{0}\in\{1,2\}
with
s_{q_{0}}=0
. Together with (
3.63
), this implies that
K(j_{i,q_{0}})(\kappa)
only depends on
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
.

We have proved the claim, and if we assume without loss of generality that
K(j_{i,1})(\kappa)
only depends on
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
we proceed as above and use the constraint on
x_{i}+K(j_{i,1})(\kappa)
to obtain

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p}\sum_{x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left\langle x_{i}\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{|x_{i}+K(j_{i,1})(\kappa)|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}\lesssim\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p}L^{d}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}}.

(3.64)

We now turn to the estimate for
B_{4}
. We use the claim we have proved to estimate
B_{1}
and without loss of generality we assume that
K(j_{i,1})(\kappa)
only depends on
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
. Since this also holds for
K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))(\kappa)
and since
K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))=K(j_{i})+K(j_{i,1})+K(j_{i,2})
, the quantity
\mathcal{K}\vcentcolon=x_{i}+K(j_{i,2})(\kappa)
only depends on
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
. We now have

\displaystyle B_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathcal{K}|\geq\varepsilon^{\gamma}}}{\left\langle x_{i}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}}\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\left(\xi+\omega^{\rho}_{\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})}\right)\right|^{p}}.

As proved in the proof of Lemma
3.10
, if
\mathtt{int}^{-1}(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))=\emptyset
then
\omega^{\rho}_{\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})}
is of the form

\displaystyle\omega^{\rho}_{\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})}=\varepsilon^{-2}\Delta_{\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})}+F_{i},

where
F_{i}
is a function of
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
. Moreover, (
2.19
) implies that

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})}

\displaystyle=\iota_{0}\left\langle K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))(\kappa)\right\rangle-\iota_{1}\left\langle x_{i}\right\rangle-\iota_{2}\left\langle K(j_{i,1})(\kappa)\right\rangle-\iota_{3}\left\langle K(j_{i,2})(\kappa)\right\rangle,

for some
\iota_{0},\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}
. Since both
K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))(\kappa)
and
K(j_{i,1})(\kappa)
only depend on
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\omega^{\rho}_{\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})}=\varepsilon^{-2}\left(-\iota_{1}\left\langle x_{i}\right\rangle-\iota_{3}\left\langle\mathcal{K}-x_{i}\right\rangle\right)+\tilde{F}_{i},

where
\tilde{F}_{i}
is a function of
(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime\prime}}
and
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}
. Therefore we have
B_{4}\lesssim\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\Theta_{\varepsilon}(x)
where the function
\Theta_{\varepsilon}
is defined on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\displaystyle\Theta_{\varepsilon}(x)\vcentcolon=\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{|\mathcal{K}|\geq\varepsilon^{\gamma}}}{\left\langle x\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}}\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\left(\varepsilon^{-2}\left(-\iota_{1}\left\langle x\right\rangle-\iota_{3}\left\langle\mathcal{K}-x\right\rangle\right)+\xi+\tilde{F}_{i}\right)\right|^{p}}.

Since
|\nabla\Theta_{\varepsilon}(x)|\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}\left\langle x\right\rangle^{-{\frac{p}{2}}}
and
p>2d
we obtain from (
3.60
) the bound

\displaystyle B_{4}\lesssim L^{d-1}\varepsilon^{-2}+L^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Theta_{\varepsilon}.

Using
L=\varepsilon^{-\beta}
for the first term and Lemma
3.13
below for the integral of
\Theta_{\varepsilon}
we finally obtain

\displaystyle B_{4}\lesssim L^{d}\varepsilon^{\beta-2}+L^{d}\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2-\gamma}.

Recall that
\mathcal{K}=K(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))(\kappa)-K(j_{i,2})(\kappa)
, which together with
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)
implies that
|\mathcal{K}|\leq(1+n(C))R
. Together with (
3.29
) this implies
\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}\lesssim|\ln\varepsilon|^{3\left(\frac{p}{2}-d+2\right)}
and thus

\displaystyle B_{4}\lesssim L^{d}\varepsilon^{\beta-2}+L^{d}|\ln\varepsilon|^{3\left(\frac{p}{2}-d+2\right)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2-\gamma}.

Together with (
3.62
) and (
3.64
) we have proved that in the case
\mathbf{C5}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{i}\in\frac{1}{L}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{i}}\lesssim L^{d}\varepsilon^{\beta-2}+L^{d}|\ln\varepsilon|^{3\left(\frac{p}{2}-d+2\right)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2-\gamma}+\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p}L^{d}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}}.

(3.65)

Using again (
3.29
) to bound
n_{T}
and and
\varepsilon\leq\delta
we have

\displaystyle L^{d}|\ln\varepsilon|^{3\left(\frac{p}{2}-d+2\right)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2-\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p}L^{d}|\ln\varepsilon|^{3\left(\frac{p}{2}-d+3\right)}\varepsilon^{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p}L^{d}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}},

if
0<\alpha_{0}<1-\gamma
and where the implicit constant depends only on
p
and
d
. Similarly, if
0<\alpha_{0}<\beta-2
and if
\varepsilon
is small enough compared to
\delta
then
|\ln\varepsilon|^{3p}\varepsilon^{\beta-2-\alpha_{0}}\leq\delta^{p}
which shows that
L^{d}\varepsilon^{\beta-2}\leq\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p}L^{d}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}}
. Therefore the three terms on the RHS of (
3.65
) are bounded by the third one, which thus proves (
3.61
) in the case
\mathbf{C5}
and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We conclude this section by proving the technical lemma used in the previous proof.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
p>2d
,
A\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
,
B\in\mathbb{R}
,
\varepsilon>0
,
\iota\in\{\pm\}
and
\mathcal{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
. If
\iota=-
, assume that
|\mathcal{K}|\geq\varepsilon^{\gamma}
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}(\langle x\rangle+\iota\langle x-\mathcal{K}\rangle+B)\right|^{p}}\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}|A|^{1-p}\varepsilon^{2-\gamma},

where the implicit constant only depends on
p
and
d
.

Proof.

We set

\displaystyle\Upsilon(x)\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}(\langle x\rangle+\iota\langle x-\mathcal{K}\rangle+B)\right|^{p}}.

Since
\mathcal{K}\neq 0
we have the orthogonal decomposition
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\mathrm{Vect}(\mathcal{K})\overset{\perp}{\oplus}\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
and we can consider the change of variables defined as the inverse of

\displaystyle(x_{\mathcal{K}},r,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-2}\longmapsto x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\in\mathrm{Vect}(\mathcal{K})\overset{\perp}{\oplus}\mathbb{R}^{d-1},

where we also used
d\geq 3
to define the polar change of variables in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
. We obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-2}}\Upsilon\left(x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\right)r^{d-2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\omega\right)\mathrm{d}x_{\mathcal{K}}.

We recall that
\left\langle x\right\rangle=\sqrt{m+|x|^{2}}
, so that

\displaystyle\Upsilon\left(x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{-\frac{p}{4}}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\iota\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+B\right)\right|^{-p}.

Note that this expression does not depend on
\omega
anymore, so that the integral over
\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
gives a constant depending on
d
. Moreover, for
x_{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{R}
fixed we define the function
\varphi
by

\displaystyle\varphi(r)=\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}(r)=\frac{r\varphi(r)}{\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

we can perform the change of variables
y=\varphi(r)
in the integral over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-2}}\Upsilon\left(x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\right)r^{d-2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\omega

(3.66)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\iota\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+B\right)\right|^{-p}

\displaystyle\times r^{d-3}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{p}{4}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}y^{-1}\mathrm{d}y.

In the case
\iota=+
, (
3.66
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-2}}\Upsilon\left(x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\right)r^{d-2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\omega

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(y+B\right)\right|^{-p}r^{d-3}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{p}{4}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}y^{-1}\mathrm{d}y.

We use the obvious facts

\displaystyle r\leq\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq y

to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-2}}\Upsilon\left(x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\right)r^{d-2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\omega

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(y+B\right)\right|^{-p}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}{2}}\mathrm{d}y.

Since
-\frac{p}{2}+d-2<0
we have
\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}{2}}\leq\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}{2}}
and therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{-p+d-2}{2}}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathcal{K}}\right)\left(\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(y+B\right)\right|^{-p}\mathrm{d}y\right).

Since
-\frac{p}{2}+d-2<-2
the integral over
x_{\mathcal{K}}
is finite and by performing the change of variable
z=\frac{y}{\varepsilon^{2}A}
in the second integral we finally obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}|A|^{1-p}.

(3.67)

In the case
\iota=-
, we use the key identity

\displaystyle\left\langle a\right\rangle-\left\langle b\right\rangle=\frac{|a|^{2}-|b|^{2}}{\left\langle a\right\rangle+\left\langle b\right\rangle}

(3.68)

which in our context becomes

\displaystyle\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2|\mathcal{K}|x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|^{2}}{y}.

Therefore, in the case
\iota=-
(
3.66
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-2}}\Upsilon\left(x_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\mathcal{K}}{|\mathcal{K}|}+r\omega\right)r^{d-2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\omega

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\frac{2|\mathcal{K}|x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|^{2}}{y}+B\right)\right|^{-p}r^{d-3}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{p}{4}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}y^{-1}\mathrm{d}y.

Since
r\leq\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
we have

\displaystyle r^{d-3}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1-\frac{p}{2}}{2}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}y^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim y^{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}{2}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}y^{\frac{p}{2}-d+1},

where we also introduced artificially the factor
y^{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}
. Since the triangle inequality
\left\langle a+b\right\rangle\lesssim\left\langle a\right\rangle+\left\langle b\right\rangle
implies

\displaystyle\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad y\lesssim\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle,

we obtain

\displaystyle\left(m+x_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}{2}}\left(m+(x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|)^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}y^{\frac{p}{2}-d+1}\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}.

Combining the above estimates and integrating first in
x_{\mathcal{K}}
and then in
y
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|A+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\frac{2|\mathcal{K}|x_{\mathcal{K}}-|\mathcal{K}|^{2}}{y}+B\right)\right|^{-p}\mathrm{d}x_{\mathcal{K}}\right)y^{-\frac{p}{2}+d-2}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}|A|^{1-p}\varepsilon^{2}|\mathcal{K}|^{-1}\int_{\varphi(0)}^{+\infty}y^{-\frac{p}{2}+d-1}\mathrm{d}y

where the inner integral was estimated thanks to the change of variables
z=\frac{2|\mathcal{K}|}{\varepsilon^{2}Ay}x_{\mathcal{K}}
. Using now
-\frac{p}{2}+d-1<-1
and
\varphi(0)>0
we finally obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}|A|^{1-p}\varepsilon^{2}|\mathcal{K}|^{-1}.

(3.69)

Since
1\lesssim|\mathcal{K}|^{-1}\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}
, we can combine (
3.67
) and (
3.69
) and obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{K}\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{2}-d+2}|A|^{1-p}\varepsilon^{2-\gamma},

where we also used
|K|^{-1}\leq\varepsilon^{-\gamma}
. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

Note that Lemmas
3.12
and
3.13
are the only place where we actually use the exact expression of the Klein-Gordon dispersion relation, in particular for the identity (
3.68
).

3.2.6
Proof of Proposition
3.3

We are now ready to conclude the proof of Proposition
3.3
.

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

We start from the estimate (
3.57
) for
A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\xi)
. Thanks to the last point of Lemma
3.9
, we can set

\displaystyle i_{0}\vcentcolon=\max\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket~\middle|~\text{$\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)$ and $\mathtt{int}^{-1}(\mathtt{parent}(j_{i}))=\emptyset$}\right\},

and rewrite (
3.57
) as

\displaystyle A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\xi)

(3.70)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}L^{-\frac{nd}{2}}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p\left(n_{T}-n^{\prime\prime}_{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}-n^{\prime\prime}}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\left\langle k_{j}\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{x_{n^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{n^{\prime\prime}}}\left(\cdots\left(\sum_{x_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{i_{0}}}R_{i_{0}}\right)\cdots\right)\right).

where we used the notation

\displaystyle R_{i_{0}}=\sum_{x_{i_{0}-1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{i_{0}-1}}\left(\cdots\left(\sum_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{1}}\right)\cdots\right).

We estimate successively each sum in
R_{i_{0}}
using (
3.61
) and obtain

\displaystyle|R_{i_{0}}|\leq\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d(i_{0}-1)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{pn^{\prime\prime}_{T,i_{0}}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain},i_{0}}},

where

\displaystyle n^{\prime\prime}_{T,i_{0}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,i_{0}-1\rrbracket~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in cases $\mathbf{C2}$, $\mathbf{C3}$, $\mathbf{C4}$ or $\mathbf{C5}$}\right\},

\displaystyle n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain},i_{0}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,i_{0}-1\rrbracket~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in cases $\mathbf{C2}$ or $\mathbf{C5}$}\right\}.

By plugging this into (
3.70
) we obtain

\displaystyle A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\xi)

(3.71)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d\left(i_{0}-1-\frac{n}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p\left(n_{T}-n^{\prime\prime}_{T}+n^{\prime\prime}_{T,i_{0}}\right)}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain},i_{0}}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}-n^{\prime\prime}}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\left\langle k_{j}\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{x_{n^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{n^{\prime\prime}}}\left(\cdots\left(\sum_{x_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{i_{0}}}\right)\cdots\right)\right).

We now consider the integral over
\mathbb{R}
in (
3.35
) and use Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\xi)^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\xi\right|}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{-p^{\star}}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R})}\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{p^{\star}-1}A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\bullet)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{\frac{1}{pp^{\prime}}}\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{p^{\star}-1}A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\bullet)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})},

(3.72)

where the bullet
\bullet
is the
\xi
variable, and where
p^{\star}\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)
. Using (
3.71
) and the fact that the
\tilde{A}_{j_{i}}
for
i>i_{0}
don’t depend on
\xi
(recall (
3.55
) and the definition of
i_{0}
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{p^{\star}-1}A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\bullet)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d\left(i_{0}-1-\frac{n}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p\left(n_{T}-n^{\prime\prime}_{T}+n^{\prime\prime}_{T,i_{0}}\right)}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain},i_{0}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}-n^{\prime\prime}}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\left\langle k_{j}\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sum_{x_{n^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{n^{\prime\prime}}}\left(\cdots\left(\sum_{x_{i_{0}+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{i_{0}+1}}\left(\sum_{x_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\xi\right|^{p(p^{\star}-1)}\tilde{A}_{j_{i_{0}}}\mathrm{d}\xi\right)\right)\cdots\right)\right).

The inner integral is estimated using (
3.55
), the definition of
i_{0}
and Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\xi\right|^{p(p^{\star}-1)}\tilde{A}_{j_{i_{0}}}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{x_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left\langle x_{i_{0}}\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{p(p^{\star}-1)}\right\|_{L^{2p}(\mathbb{R})}\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\left(\bullet-\omega^{\rho}_{\mathtt{parent}(j)}\right)\right|^{-p}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{2p-1}}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim L^{d}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p-\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}},

where we also used (
3.58
) and
p>2d
. Moreover, the remaining successive sums above (i.e over
x_{i}
for
i_{0}+1\leq i\leq n^{\prime\prime}
) are again estimated using (
3.61
). This implies

\displaystyle\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{p^{\star}-1}A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\bullet)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d\left(n^{\prime\prime}-\frac{n}{2}\right)}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{pn_{T}-\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}}\sum_{(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}-n^{\prime\prime}}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus(\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)\sqcup\{r_{1}\})}\left\langle k_{j}\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

where

\displaystyle n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}\vcentcolon=\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\setminus\{i_{0}\}~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in cases $\mathbf{C2}$ or $\mathbf{C5}$}\right\},

where we used that

\displaystyle\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\setminus\{i_{0}\}~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in cases $\mathbf{C2}$, $\mathbf{C3}$, $\mathbf{C4}$ or $\mathbf{C5}$}\right\}=n_{T}^{\prime\prime}-1,

since the cases
\mathbf{C2}
,
\mathbf{C3}
,
\mathbf{C4}
or
\mathbf{C5}
cover
\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
and since
\mathtt{parent}(j_{i_{0}})\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
by definition of
i_{0}
. We use (
3.58
) and
p>2d
to finally bound the sum over the
k_{j}
’s and we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\bullet\right|^{p^{\star}-1}A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\bullet)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{n_{T}-\frac{1}{pp^{\prime}}}\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{p}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}}.

By plugging this into (
3.72
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{C}^{\rho}(k,\xi)^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\xi\right|}\mathrm{d}\xi\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{n_{T}}\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{p}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}}.

We plug this into (
3.35
) and obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\left|\widehat{F^{\rho}_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{n_{T}}\varepsilon^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{p}n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B}}.

By using
\varepsilon\leq\delta
and
n=n_{B}+2n_{T}
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\left|\widehat{F^{\rho}_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}\left(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B}\right)},

where we defined
\alpha_{1}\vcentcolon=\min\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{\alpha_{0}}{p}\right)
. Recalling (
3.34
) and using
\#\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(C))\leq n_{T}!\lesssim n_{T}^{n/2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\#\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(C))\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}\left(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}\left(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B}\right)}.

(3.73)

In order to deduce (
3.30
) from (
3.73
), we manipulate the exponent
n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B}
. For that we define

\displaystyle n^{1}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in case $\mathbf{C5}$ and $\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{P}_{1}$}\right\},

\displaystyle n^{2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in case $\mathbf{C5}$ and $\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{P}_{2}$}\right\}+\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket~\middle|~\text{$j_{i}$ in case $\mathbf{C2}$}\right\}.

We first estimate
n^{1}
. Since a node in
\mathcal{P}_{1}
belongs either to
\mathcal{N}_{B}(C)
,
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
or
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{high}}(C)
we have

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{P}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq n_{B}+n_{\mathrm{low}}+\#\left(\mathcal{P}_{1}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{high}}(C)\right)

\displaystyle\leq n_{B}+n_{\mathrm{low}}+\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket~\middle|~\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{high}}(C)\right\},

where we have also used the natural bijection between elements of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and their unique children in
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
. Since to any
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
such that
\mathtt{int}^{-1}(j)\neq 0
we can injectively associate a unique binary node, we have

\displaystyle\#\left\{i\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket~\middle|~\mathtt{parent}(j_{i})\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{high}}(C)\right\}\leq n^{1}+n_{B}.

We have proven that

\displaystyle n^{1}\geq\#\mathcal{P}_{1}-2n_{B}-n_{\mathrm{low}}.

(3.74)

Together with (
3.47
), the exact same arguments implies that
\#\mathcal{P}_{2}\leq n^{2}+n_{B}
so that

\displaystyle n^{2}\geq\#\mathcal{P}_{2}-n_{B}.

(3.75)

Since we don’t know if
j_{i_{0}}
is in one of the cases
\mathbf{C2}
or
\mathbf{C5}
we have
n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}\geq n^{1}+n^{2}-1
which in particular implies
2n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}\geq n^{1}+2n^{2}-2
(simply using
n^{1}\leq 2n^{1}
). Together with (
3.74
) and (
3.75
) this implies

\displaystyle 2(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B})

\displaystyle\geq\#\mathcal{P}_{1}+2\#\mathcal{P}_{2}-2n_{B}-n_{\mathrm{low}}-2

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{2}-2n_{B}-n_{\mathrm{low}}-2,

where we used (
3.48
). Introducing artificially
n_{\mathrm{scb}}
we obtain

\displaystyle 2(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n-2n_{\mathrm{scb}}}{2}-2n_{B}-(n_{\mathrm{low}}-n_{\mathrm{scb}})-2.

We now use the high nodes regime assumption (
3.27
) to bound
-2n_{B}
from below, we obtain:

\displaystyle 2(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{10}\left(n-2n_{\mathrm{scb}}\right)+3(n_{\mathrm{low}}-n_{\mathrm{scb}})-2

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{10}\left(n-2n_{\mathrm{scb}}\right)-2

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n-2n_{\mathrm{scb}}}{10},

where we used
n_{\mathrm{low}}-n_{\mathrm{scb}}\geq 0
and (
3.28
). Recalling (
3.73
), this bound on
n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+n_{B}
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}\left(\frac{n}{2}-n_{\mathrm{scb}}\right)},

where we have set
\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}\vcentcolon=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{10}
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
3.3
.
∎

We conclude Section
3.2
by combining the results of Propositions
3.2
and
3.3
, which together leads to Proposition
3.1
(after having set
\alpha\vcentcolon=\min\left(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}\right)
).

3.3
Time derivative of
X_{n}

In order to bound in a high probability setting the
L^{\infty}
norm in time of
X_{n}
, we need to control in law its derivative in time. For this we state the following propositions.

Proposition 3.5
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\eta\in\{0,1\}
and
\iota\in\{\pm\}
, we have

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 13.87144pt\hskip-13.87144pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}X^{\eta,\iota}_{n}$}\hskip-13.87144pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.87144pt}=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\eta,\iota}}\hbox{\hskip 21.38728pt\hskip-21.38728pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}F_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-21.38728pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.38728pt},

together with the decomposition

\partial_{t}F_{(A,\varphi,c)}

⋀

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\{-1\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 26.44382pt\hskip-26.44382pt\hbox{$F_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.44382pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.44382pt},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)\vcentcolon=\left\{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)~\middle|~\nexists\,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A),\;j^{\prime}>j\right\}

(3.76)

and where
\hbox{\hskip 26.44382pt\hskip-26.44382pt\hbox{$F_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.44382pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.44382pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)
is defined for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
by

•

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.4446pt\hskip-21.4446pt\hbox{$F_{(A,\varphi,c),-1}$}\hskip-21.4446pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.4446pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}}}{L^{\frac{nd}{2}}}(-i)^{n_{T}(A)+1}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(A)}\Omega_{-1}e^{i\Omega_{-1}\varepsilon^{-2}t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(A)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

•

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 26.44382pt\hskip-26.44382pt\hbox{$F_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.44382pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.44382pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}+2}}{L^{\frac{nd}{2}}}(-i)^{n_{T}(A)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(A)}e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(A)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

where

\displaystyle I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\vcentcolon=\left\{(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}\in[0,t]^{n_{T}-1}~\middle|~j\leq j^{\prime}\Rightarrow t_{j}\leq t_{j^{\prime}}\right\}.

(3.77)

Proof.

By directly differentiating (
2.14
) we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}X^{\eta,\iota}_{n}=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\eta,\iota}}\partial_{t}F_{(A,\varphi,c)}.

Since
\hbox{\hskip 21.38728pt\hskip-21.38728pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}F_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-21.38728pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.38728pt}=\partial_{t}\hbox{\hskip 16.97235pt\hskip-16.97235pt\hbox{$F_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.97235pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.97235pt}
we want to differentiate with respect to time the formula (
2.31
). Note that
t
appears in
e^{i\Omega_{-1}t}
and in the time domain
I_{A}(t)
. In order to obtain the time derivative of the integral over
I_{A}(t)
, we can make use of (
3.32
) which implies

\displaystyle\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A))}\int_{0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T})}\leq t}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{T}}f_{\rho(i)}\left(t_{\rho(i)}\right)\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)},

for some functions
f_{j}
. Rewriting the integrals on the RHS as successive time integrals we thus obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A))}f_{\rho(n_{T})}(t)\int_{0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}-1)}\leq t}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{T}-1}f_{\rho(i)}\left(t_{\rho(i)}\right)\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}.

By definition of the set
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)
(see (
3.76
)), the following map is a bijection

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigsqcup_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)}\Big(\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}\times\mathfrak{M}\left(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\rho\qquad

\displaystyle\longmapsto\hskip 68.28644pt\left(\rho(n_{T}),\rho_{|_{\left\llbracket 1,n_{T}-1\right\rrbracket}}\right)

and therefore we can perform a change of variables in the sum over
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A))
and get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)}f_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\sum_{\bar{\rho}\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\})}\int_{0\leq t_{\bar{\rho}(1)}<\cdots<t_{\bar{\rho}(n_{T}-1)}\leq t}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{T}-1}f_{\bar{\rho}(i)}\left(t_{\bar{\rho}(i)}\right)\mathrm{d}t_{\bar{\rho}(i)}.

Using again the formula (
3.32
) but now applied to the sets
\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\})
we finally obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)=\sum_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)}f_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j},

where
I^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}_{A}(t)
is defined in (
3.77
). Applying this formula with
f_{j}(t_{j})=e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}
allows us to differentiate (
2.31
) with respect to time and conclude the proof of the proposition, after the usual dilation in time.
∎

From this proposition and the usual Wick formula, we obtain the following decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.67131pt\hskip-11.67131pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}X_{n}^{\eta}$}\hskip-11.67131pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.67131pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}_{n,n}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\{-1\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)\\
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\in\{-1\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\hbox{\hskip 26.00037pt\hskip-26.00037pt\hbox{$F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.00037pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.00037pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k),

(3.78)

where
\hbox{\hskip 26.00037pt\hskip-26.00037pt\hbox{$F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.00037pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.00037pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)
is defined by

•

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.00192pt\hskip-16.00192pt\hbox{$F_{C,-1,-1}$}\hskip-16.00192pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.00192pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}}}{L^{\frac{nd}{2}}}(-i)^{n_{T}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A)\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})e^{i\Omega_{-1}(C)\varepsilon^{-2}t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

•

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.00114pt\hskip-21.00114pt\hbox{$F_{C,-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-21.00114pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.00114pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}+2}}{L^{\frac{nd}{2}}}(-i)^{n_{T}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A)e^{i(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(A^{\prime}))t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{I^{-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

where
I^{-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{C}(t)=I_{A}(t)\times I_{A^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)
,

•

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.00114pt\hskip-21.00114pt\hbox{$F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}$}\hskip-21.00114pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.00114pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}+2}}{L^{\frac{nd}{2}}}(-i)^{n_{T}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})e^{i(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(A))t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

where
I^{j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}_{C}(t)=I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\times I_{A^{\prime}}(t)
,

•

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 26.00037pt\hskip-26.00037pt\hbox{$F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.00037pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.00037pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}+4}}{L^{\frac{nd}{2}}}(-i)^{n_{T}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)}e^{i(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(A)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(A^{\prime}))t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\times\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)}.

where
I^{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{C}(t)=I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\times I^{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{A^{\prime}}(t)
.

Remark 3.10
.

Based on the fact that the cardinal of
N_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A)
is bounded by
\frac{n(A)}{2}
and thus grows linearly in
n(A)
the result of Lemma
2.4
is unchanged. More precisely we have

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{n,q}\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left(\frac{n}{2}+2-q\right)!.

where
\tilde{c}_{n,q}
be the cardinal of the set

\displaystyle\left\{(C,j_{1},j_{2})~\middle|~C\in\mathcal{C}_{n_{1},n_{2}},\;n_{1}+n_{2}=n,\;n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)=q,\;j_{1}\in\{-1\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A_{1}),\;j_{2}\in\{-1\}\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A_{2})\right\}

where
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are the two trees in
C
.

The following proposition contains the key estimate satisfied by

F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}

⋀

.

Proposition 3.6
.

There exists
\Lambda>0
such that for all
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}_{n,n}
and for all
(j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}})\in(\{-1\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A))\times(\{-1\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{max}}(A^{\prime}))
(where
A
and
A^{\prime}
are the two trees in
C
) we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left|\hbox{\hskip 26.00037pt\hskip-26.00037pt\hbox{$F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.00037pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.00037pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{n(C)}{2}}.

Moreover, there exists
\alpha>0
such that if
C
satisfies

\displaystyle n(C)-2n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)\geq 80,\qquad n(c)\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{c}

then for any
\varepsilon\leq\delta
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left|\hbox{\hskip 26.00037pt\hskip-26.00037pt\hbox{$F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.00037pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.00037pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{n(C)}{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}\left(\frac{n(C)}{2}-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)\right)}.

(3.79)

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is identical to the one of Proposition
3.1
, i.e we consider the low nodes regime and the high nodes regime. The only two differences between the formulas for

F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}

⋀

and the formula for

F_{C}

⋀

are the presence of the terms
\Omega_{-1}(A)
or
\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})
and the fact that there might be one or two oscillating integrals less. However since the factors
\Omega_{-1}(A)
and
\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})
grow linearly in
n(C)
(thanks to the compact support property) and since the missing oscillating integrals are automatically compensated by a better
\varepsilon
power, the proofs of Section
3.1
and
3.2
apply without any change (and produce even better estimates since
\varepsilon\leq\delta
).
∎

3.4
Proof of Corollary
3.1

We conclude Section
3
by proving its main result, i.e. Corollary
3.1
.

Proof.

First, we begin with proving the existence of
\Lambda>0
such that for any
n\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
,
t\in[0,\delta]
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\widehat{X^{\eta}_{n}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|^{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\widehat{\partial_{t}X^{\eta}_{n}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|^{2}\right)\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{n}{2}}.

(3.80)

We explain how to get the estimate for the first expectation, which is deduced from identity (
2.45
), Proposition
3.1
and Lemma
2.4
. The estimate for the latter follows analogously using the identity (
3.78
), Proposition
3.6
and Remark
3.10
instead.

For
n\leq 80
, the statement follows straightforwardly. For
n>80
, we observe that

\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}_{n,n}}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|=\sum_{0\leq q\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-40}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\in\mathcal{C}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}_{n,n}\\
n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)=q\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|+\sum_{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-40<q\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\in\mathcal{C}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}_{n,n}\\
n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)=q\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

The estimate of the latter sum in the above right hand side is again straightforward using (
3.1
) and Lemma
2.4
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-40<q\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\in\mathcal{C}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}_{n,n}\\
n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)=q\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{n}{2}+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\sum_{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-q<40}\Big(\frac{n}{2}+2-q\Big)!

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{n}{2}+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\sum_{m\leq 40}(m+2)!

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{n}{2}+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}.

For the first sum, we use the improved bound (
3.2
) and get

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq q\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-40}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\in\mathcal{C}^{\eta,\eta,+,-}_{n,n}\\
n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)=q\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{n}{2}+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\sum_{0\leq q\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor-40}\varepsilon^{\alpha(\frac{n}{2}-q)}\Big(\frac{n}{2}+2-q\Big)!

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{n}{2}+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\sum_{40\leq m\leq\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\varepsilon^{\alpha m}(m+3)!.

Stirling’s approximation of factorials and the restriction
n\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
then shows that the last sum is bounded by
(n\varepsilon^{\alpha})^{n}\leq(|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}\varepsilon^{\alpha})^{n}
which is indeed uniformly bounded when
\varepsilon
goes to 0. Therefore we have proved (
3.80
).

Second, we observe that the identities (
2.45
) and (
3.78
), the formula (
2.40
) and the equivalent ones for

F_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}

⋀

, together with the assumption on the functions
M^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}
to be supported in
B(0,R)
imply that the functions

\displaystyle k\longmapsto\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\widehat{X^{\eta}_{n}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|^{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad k\longmapsto\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\widehat{\partial_{t}X^{\eta}_{n}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|^{2}\right)

are actually supported in a ball of
\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
of radius
\mathcal{O}(n)
. This implies that for any
s\geq 0
, we can get from (
3.80
) the following bound, for some new positive constant
\Lambda
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|X^{\eta}_{n}(t,\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\partial_{t}X^{\eta}_{n}(t,\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\right)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{n}{2}+1}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}n^{2s+d}L^{d}.

We successively apply Sobolev injection on
[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
, Fubini’s theorem and integrate the above estimate in time to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left( \sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\left\|X^{\eta}_{n}(t,\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left(\|X_{n}\|^{2}_{H^{1}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta],H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{-2}\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|X^{\eta}_{n}(t,\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\partial_{t}X^{\eta}_{n}(t,\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{n}{2}+1}n^{2s+d}L^{d+\frac{2}{\beta}},

where we used
\varepsilon=L^{-1/\beta}
. Finally, Markov’s inequality and the restriction
n\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
, allows us to conclude that for
a\vcentcolon=(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{n}{2}+1}L^{d+\frac{2}{\beta}+2A}

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\left\|X^{\eta}_{n}(t,\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\geq a\right)\leq L^{-A},

which concludes the proof of the corollary.
∎

4
Fixed point argument

In this section we show how to construct a true solution to the fixed point (
1.12
). More precisely, we will prove the following.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
s>d/2
and
A>0
be fixed. There exists a set
\mathcal{E}_{L,A}
of probability larger than
1-L^{-A}
on which there exists a unique solution
(X^{0},X^{1})\in\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))
to (
1.12
) that admits a decomposition of the form

X^{\eta}=\sum_{n\leq N(L)}X^{\eta}_{n}+v^{\eta}

(4.1)

where
N(L)\vcentcolon=\lfloor\log L\rfloor
,
X^{\eta}_{n}
is defined in Definition
2.2
and satisfies (
3.3
) and
v
satisfies

\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\lesssim L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}.

We use for brevity the notation

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{\leq N}\vcentcolon=\sum_{n=0}^{N}X_{n}^{\eta}.

(4.2)

We want
X^{\eta}
defined by (
4.1
) to solve

\displaystyle X^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle=X^{\eta}_{0}+\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\left(B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(t)\right)-B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(0,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{0},X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-i\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}}C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}(\tau)\right)\mathrm{d}\tau,

where we already replaced
X^{\eta}_{\rm in}
by
X^{\eta}_{0}
(cf (
2.3
)). By plugging the ansatz (
4.1
) into this equation, we obtain the following fixed point formulation for the remainder
v
:

\displaystyle v^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{S}^{\eta}+\mathfrak{L}(v)^{\eta}+\mathfrak{N}(v)^{\eta},

(4.3)

where the source term, linear term and nonlinear term are defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-X^{\eta}_{\leq N(L)}(t)+X^{\eta}_{0}+\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\left(B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(t)\right)-B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(0,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{0},X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{0}\right)\right)

(4.4)

\displaystyle-i\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}}C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau)\right)\mathrm{d}\tau,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}(v)^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\left(B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(t),v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(t)\right)+B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(t)\right)\right)

(4.5)

\displaystyle-i\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}}\bigg(C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),v^{\eta,\iota_{3}}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle+C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle+C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,v^{\eta,\iota_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau)\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}\tau,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}(v)^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}(t),v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(t)\right)

(4.6)

\displaystyle-i\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}}\bigg(C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(\tau),v^{\eta,\iota_{3}}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle+C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,v^{\eta,\iota_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),v^{\eta,\iota_{3}}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle+C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,v^{\eta,\iota_{1}}(\tau),v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle+C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,v^{\eta,\iota_{1}}(\tau),v^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(\tau),v^{\eta,\iota_{3}}(\tau)\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}\tau.

As in
[
DH23b
]
, the main obstructions to the resolution of (
4.3
) are that the source term must be small (and not diverging with
L
as
L^{d/2}
) and that the linear operator
\mathfrak{L}
must be such that
\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}
is invertible (where
\mathrm{Id}
denotes the identity operator of
\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta],H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))
). A naive use of (
3.3
) shows that
\mathfrak{L}
has operator norm of order
L^{d+2A}
, thus implying that
\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}
is
a priori
not invertible. However, by relying once again on tree expansions, we will prove that its
N(L)
-th iterate
\mathfrak{L}^{N(L)}
does have a small operator norm. This will imply that
\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}^{N(L)}
is invertible and thus that
\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}
is in fact invertible, thanks to the formula

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}=\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}^{N(L)}\right)^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{N(L)-1}\mathfrak{L}^{n}.

(4.7)

Once this is done, one can solve (
4.3
) by showing that the mapping
v\longmapsto\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathfrak{S}+\mathfrak{N}(v)\right)
is a contraction from a ball of small radius to itself.
This is the content of the following proposition, which would then implies Theorem
4.1
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
s>d/2
be fixed. There exists
L_{0}=L_{0}(\beta,d)
such that for any
L\geq L_{0}
and any
A>0
, there exists a set
\mathcal{E}_{L,A}
of probability
\geq 1-L^{-A}
such that the mapping

v\longmapsto\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathfrak{S}+\mathfrak{N}(v)\right)

is a contraction from the set

\displaystyle\left\{v\in\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))~\middle|~\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda_{0}L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}\right\}

to itself, where
\Lambda_{0}\geq 1
is some fixed constant independent of
R,L
.

This section is organized as follows: in Section
4.1
we estimate the operator norm of
\mathfrak{L}^{N}
for any positive integer
N
by introducing an adapted diagrammatic extending the one of Section
2
; Section
4.2
will be dedicated to the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

4.1
Linear estimates

In this section, we estimate the operator norm of the
N
-th iterate
\mathfrak{L}^{N}
. For this, we represent the action of the iterates
\mathfrak{L}^{k}
with trees expansions.

4.1.1
Representation of the iterates with linear trees

Compared to the objects introduced in Section
2.2
, we introduce a new type of leaf
\bullet
, a new type of binary nodes
B^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}
and new types of ternary nodes
T_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}
for
*\in\{{\mathrm{low-}\ell},{\mathrm{low-}m},{\mathrm{low-}r},\mathrm{high}\}
. They play roles similar to the ones they play in the diagrammatic used to bound
X_{n}
. We define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\vcentcolon=\bigsqcup_{0\leq n\leq N(L)}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n},

where the sets of trees
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
are defined in Section
2.2
. We now define what we call linear trees:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{0}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\{\bullet\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\bigsqcup_{*}\left\{T_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}\left(A,A^{\prime},A^{\mathrm{lin}}\right),T_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}\left(A,A^{\mathrm{lin}},A^{\prime}\right),T_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}\left(A^{\mathrm{lin}},A,A^{\prime}\right)~\middle|~\left(A,A^{\prime},A^{\mathrm{lin}}\right)\in\left(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\right)^{2}\times\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\sqcup\left\{B_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}\left(A,A^{\mathrm{lin}}\right),B_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}\left(A^{\mathrm{lin}},A\right)~\middle|~\left(A,A^{\mathrm{lin}}\right)\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\times\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}\right\},

for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. As opposed to the sets
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
, here the
n
in
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}
directly counts the number of linear nodes in a given linear tree. Moreover, each linear tree only has one
\bullet
leaf. Note also that the “linear” structure of a linear tree is a comb to which we attach standard trees from
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}
,
A_{1}
to
A_{4}
in the example below:
\mathrm{st}^{\mathrm{lin}}
A_{1}
*^{\mathrm{lin}}
A_{2}
\mathrm{st}^{\mathrm{lin}}
\bullet
A_{4}
A_{3}

As done in Definition
2.3
, we need to define the relevant sets of branching nodes and leaves of a given linear tree
A^{\mathrm{lin}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}
. The difference between a linear tree
A^{\mathrm{lin}}
and a normal tree only lies in the presence of the new types of nodes and leaves, so that the first six sets from Definition
2.3
can be also defined for linear trees. In addition to these, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{\text{linear standard binary nodes of $A^{\mathrm{lin}}$}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{B}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{\text{linear ternary nodes of $A^{\mathrm{lin}}$ of type $*$}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{*}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{*}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low-}\ell}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low-}m}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low-}r}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{high}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}).

The cardinals of these sets are denoted as in Definition
2.3
, for instance
n^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
is the cardinal of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
and
n_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
is the cardinal of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
. We also define

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{st}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\mathcal{N}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{\text{leaves of $A^{\mathrm{lin}}$ that are not $\bullet$}\right\}.

The size of
A^{\mathrm{lin}}
is given by
n(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\vcentcolon=n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})+2n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
, this matches with the cardinal of
\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
as it can be shown by induction. Finally, we can easily extend the definition of the sign and colour maps (recall Definition
2.6
) to elements of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}^{\mathrm{lin}}
. As before, a sign map

\displaystyle\varphi:\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\{\bullet\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\longrightarrow\{\pm\}

is arbitrary while a colour map

\displaystyle c:\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\{\bullet\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\longrightarrow\{\pm\}

can be entirely deduced from the knowledge of the root’s colour by asking that the rules defining the colour of a child with respect to the colour of its parent also apply to linear nodes. As in Definition
2.6
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n,\eta,\iota}\vcentcolon=\left\{\text{$(A,\varphi,c)$ signed and coloured linear tree}~\middle|~A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n},\;\varphi(\text{root of $A$})=\iota,\;c(\text{root of $A$})=\eta\right\},

for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\eta\in\{0,1\}
and
\iota\in\{\pm\}
. Mimicking the recursive definition of the
F_{(A,\varphi,c)}
(see Definition
2.7
), we fix
v
and define
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
by

•

First, if
(A,\varphi,c)\in\bigsqcup_{0\leq n\leq N(L)}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\eta,\iota}
, then set
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}=F_{(A,\varphi,c)}
where
F_{(A,\varphi,c)}
is defined in Definition
2.7
.

•

Second, if
(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n,\eta,\iota}
, then
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
is defined recursively as follows:

–

if
n=0
, set
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(\bullet,\iota,\eta)}=v^{\eta,\iota}
,

–

if
A=B^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}(A_{1},A_{2})
, set

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}(t)\vcentcolon=\varepsilon B^{\eta,\iota_{r}}_{\iota_{r}(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(A_{1},\iota_{r}\varphi_{|_{A_{1}}},c_{|_{A_{1}}}\right)}(t),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(A_{2},\iota_{r}\varphi_{|_{A_{2}}},c_{|_{A_{2}}}\right)}(t)\right),

where
\iota_{r}
and
\iota_{1}
,
\iota_{2}
are the signs of the root of
A
and the roots of
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
in
A
and
\eta
is the colour of the root of
A
,

–

if
A=T_{*}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A_{1},A_{2},A_{3})
, set

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(A,\varphi,c\right)}(t)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-i\iota_{r}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}C_{*,\iota_{r}(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}^{\eta,\iota_{r}}\left(\tau,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(A_{1},\iota_{r}\varphi_{|_{A_{1}}},c_{|_{A_{1}}}\right)}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(A_{2},\iota_{r}\varphi_{|_{A_{2}}},c_{|_{A_{2}}}\right)}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(A_{3},\iota_{r}\varphi_{|_{A_{3}}},c_{|_{A_{3}}}\right)}(\tau)\right)\mathrm{d}\tau,

where
\iota_{r}
and
\iota_{1}
,
\iota_{2}
,
\iota_{3}
are the signs of the root of
A
and the roots of
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,
A_{3}
in
A
, and
\eta
is the colour of the root of
A
.

The following proposition shows how we represent the iterates of the linear operator
\mathfrak{L}
in terms of the
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\eta\in\{0,1\}
and
\iota\in\{\pm\}
we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\eta,\iota}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n,\eta,\iota}}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}.

Proof.

We prove the proposition by induction over
n\in\mathbb{N}
. For
n=0
, the proposition holds according to the definition of
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(\bullet,\iota,\eta)}
above. If it holds for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then we compute
\mathfrak{L}^{n+1}(v)
according to the definition (
4.5
) of
\mathfrak{L}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{n+1}(v)^{\eta,\iota}(t)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\left(B^{\eta,\iota}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(t),\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(t)\right)+B^{\eta,\iota}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(t)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-i\iota\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}}\bigg(C^{\eta,\iota}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\eta,\iota_{3}}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+C^{\eta,\iota}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+C^{\eta,\iota}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\eta,\iota_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{\leq N(L)}(\tau)\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Thanks to Proposition
2.1
, (
2.15
) and the definition of
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
for
(A,\varphi,c)\in\bigsqcup_{0\leq n\leq N(L)}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\eta,\iota}
we have

\displaystyle X^{\eta^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}_{\leq N(L)}=\sum_{A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}}\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(A,\iota^{\prime})}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c_{\eta^{\prime}})},

(4.8)

where the notations
\mathrm{SM}(A,\iota)
and
c_{\eta}
are defined in the proof of Proposition
2.1
. Moreover, thanks to the induction hypothesis and (
2.15
) we also have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{n}(v)^{\eta^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}}\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(A,\iota^{\prime})}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c_{\eta^{\prime}})}.

(4.9)

Therefore, by inserting (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) into the above expression of
\mathfrak{L}^{n+1}(v)^{\eta,\iota}(t)
and after having changed variables in the sums over
\{\pm\}
from
\iota_{i}
to
\iota\iota_{i}
(which implies a similar change of variables in the sums over sign maps) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{n+1}(v)^{\eta,\iota}(t)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A^{\prime},A)\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\times\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\\
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SM}(A^{\prime},\iota_{1})\\
\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(A,\iota_{2})\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(B^{\eta,\iota}_{\iota(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime},c_{\bar{\eta}})}(t),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\iota\varphi,c_{\bar{\eta}})}(t)\right)+B^{\eta,\iota}_{\iota(\iota_{2},\iota_{1})}\left(t,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\iota\varphi,c_{\bar{\eta}})}(t),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime},c_{\bar{\eta}})}(t)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-i\iota\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}*\\
A^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\\
A^{\prime\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\\
A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}\\
\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(A,\iota_{1})\\
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{SM}(A^{\prime},\iota_{2})\\
\varphi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{SM}(A^{\prime\prime},\iota_{3})\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bigg(C^{\eta,\iota}_{*,\iota(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\iota\varphi,c_{\eta})}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime},c_{\bar{\eta}})}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime\prime},c_{\eta})}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 113.81102pt+C^{\eta,\iota}_{*,\iota(\iota_{2},\iota_{3},\iota_{1})}\left(\tau,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime},c_{\eta})}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime\prime},c_{\bar{\eta}})}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\iota\varphi,c_{\eta})}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 113.81102pt+C^{\eta,\iota}_{*,\iota(\iota_{3},\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(\tau,F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime\prime},c_{\eta})}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\iota\varphi,c_{\bar{\eta}})}(\tau),F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A^{\prime},\iota\varphi^{\prime},c_{\eta})}(\tau)\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Using now (
2.16
)-(
2.17
) and their natural equivalent for linear nodes, the recursive definition of
F^{\mathrm{lin}}
and the recursive rules defining a colour map we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{n+1}(v)^{\eta,\iota}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A^{\prime},A)\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\times\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(B^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}(A^{\prime},A),\iota)}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(B^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}(A^{\prime},A),\varphi,c_{\eta}\right)}(t)+\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(B^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}(A,A^{\prime}),\iota)}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(B^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{st}}(A,A^{\prime}),\varphi,c_{\eta}\right)}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}*\\
A^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\\
A^{\prime\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq N(L)}\\
A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(T^{\mathrm{lin}}_{*}(A,A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}),\iota)}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(T^{\mathrm{lin}}_{*}(A,A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}),\varphi,c_{\eta}\right)}(t)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left.\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(T^{\mathrm{lin}}_{*}(A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime},A),\iota)}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(T^{\mathrm{lin}}_{*}(A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime},A),\varphi,c_{\eta}\right)}(t)+\sum_{\varphi\in\mathrm{SM}(T^{\mathrm{lin}}_{*}(A^{\prime\prime},A,A^{\prime}),\iota)}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\left(T^{\mathrm{lin}}_{*}(A^{\prime\prime},A,A^{\prime}),\varphi,c_{\eta}\right)}(t)\right).

Using now again (
2.15
) and the definition of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n+1}
we finally obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{n+1}(v)^{\eta,\iota}(t)=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n+1,\eta,\iota}}F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)},

which concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

4.1.2
Fourier transform of the iterates

In this section, we give the Fourier transform of
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
. First, we extend the definition of decoration map and
k
-decoration from Definition
2.8
to linear trees, the only difference being the domain of definition and the recursive relation, which now takes into account the different definition of
\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
. More precisely a decoration map
\kappa
of
A^{\mathrm{lin}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n}
is now defined on
\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\{\bullet\}\sqcup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
and the recursive relation is

\displaystyle\kappa(j)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\sqcup\{\bullet\},\ell\leq j}\kappa(\ell).

We define
\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
to be the set of
k
-decoration of
A^{\mathrm{lin}}
such that
\kappa(\bullet)=k_{\bullet}
. We also extend Definitions
2.9
,
2.10
,
2.11
and
2.12
to the linear nodes of a linear tree. Finally, if
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})\neq\emptyset
then we define

\displaystyle\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\vcentcolon=\min\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A^{\mathrm{lin}}),

where the minimum is defined with respect to parentality order (note that the linear structure defined in Section
4.1.1
in particular implies that
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})
is totally ordered for the parentality order).

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
A
be a linear tree,
\varphi
and
c
sign and colour maps on
A
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
.

•

If
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)=\emptyset
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.97235pt\hskip-16.97235pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.97235pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.97235pt}(t,k)=\frac{1}{L^{\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\hbox{\hskip 18.74896pt\hskip-18.74896pt\hbox{$v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}$}\hskip-18.74896pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.74896pt}(t,k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 16.99387pt\hskip-16.99387pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.99387pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.99387pt}(t,k,k_{\bullet})

with
G_{(A,\varphi,c)}(t,\cdot,k_{\bullet})
defined in Fourier space by

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.99387pt\hskip-16.99387pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.99387pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.99387pt}(t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(-i)^{n_{T}(A)}\frac{\varepsilon^{n(A)}}{L^{(n(A)-1)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{-1}t}

(4.10)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

where
I_{A}(t)
is defined in Proposition
2.2
.

•

If
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)\neq\emptyset
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.97235pt\hskip-16.97235pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.97235pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.97235pt}(t,k)=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{L^{\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 18.74896pt\hskip-18.74896pt\hbox{$v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}$}\hskip-18.74896pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.74896pt}(t_{\bullet},k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 16.99387pt\hskip-16.99387pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.99387pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.99387pt}(t,t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})\mathrm{d}t_{\bullet}

with
G_{(A,\varphi,c)}(t,t_{\bullet},\cdot,k_{\bullet})
defined in Fourier space by

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.99387pt\hskip-16.99387pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.99387pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.99387pt}(t,t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)}\frac{\varepsilon^{n(A)-2}}{L^{(n(A)-1)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)}e^{i(\Omega_{-1}t}e^{i\Omega_{\bullet}t_{\bullet}}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

where we use the notations
\Omega_{\bullet}=\Omega_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}
and

I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})\vcentcolon=\Bigg\{(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}\in[0,t]^{n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)-1}\;\Bigg|\;j<j^{\prime}\Rightarrow t_{j}<t_{j^{\prime}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;\begin{aligned} &j>\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\Rightarrow t_{j}>t_{\bullet}\\
&j<\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\Rightarrow t_{j}<t_{\bullet}\end{aligned}\Bigg\}.

Proof.

We again follow the same steps as in Section
2
and compute the Fourier transform of
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
for
(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{n,\eta,\iota}
. We crucially note that the recursive definition of the
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
is identical to the one of the
F_{(A,\varphi,c)}
and therefore we can easily obtain the following formula:

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.97235pt\hskip-16.97235pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.97235pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.97235pt}(t,k)

\displaystyle=(-i)^{n_{T}(A)}\frac{\varepsilon^{n(A)}}{L^{n(A)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{-1}t}
