\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I_{A}(t)}\hbox{\hskip 26.36127pt\hskip-26.36127pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(\bullet,\varphi(\bullet),c(\bullet))}$}\hskip-26.36127pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.36127pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)},\kappa(\bullet))\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 26.18768pt\hskip-26.18768pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(\perp,\varphi(\ell),c(\ell))}$}\hskip-26.18768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.18768pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\ell)},\kappa(\ell))\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j},

where by convention
t_{-1}=t
. This formula is the generalization of (
2.31
) to the case where the leaves of a tree are allowed to depend on time and it can be proved identically. According to the definition of
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}
and (
2.9
) we have
\hbox{\hskip 26.18768pt\hskip-26.18768pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(\perp,\varphi(\ell),c(\ell))}$}\hskip-26.18768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.18768pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\ell)},\kappa(\ell))=\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell))
and
F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(\bullet,\varphi(\bullet),c(\bullet))}=v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}
and thus this formula becomes

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.97235pt\hskip-16.97235pt\hbox{$F^{\mathrm{lin}}_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-16.97235pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.97235pt}(t,k)

\displaystyle=(-i)^{n_{T}(A)}\frac{\varepsilon^{n(A)}}{L^{n(A)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{-1}t}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell))\int_{I_{A}(t)}\hbox{\hskip 18.74896pt\hskip-18.74896pt\hbox{$v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}$}\hskip-18.74896pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.74896pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)},\kappa(\bullet))\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j},

Now we distinguish cases between
\mathrm{int}(\bullet)=-1
or
\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)
. Note that due to the structure of linear trees, the first case is equivalent to
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)=\emptyset
and the second to
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)\neq\emptyset
, since the only nodes comparable to
\bullet
are linear nodes.

•

In the case
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)=\emptyset
, we have
t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}=t
and thus
\hbox{\hskip 18.74896pt\hskip-18.74896pt\hbox{$v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}$}\hskip-18.74896pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.74896pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)},\kappa(\bullet))
is factorized outside the integral over
I_{A}(t)
and using
\mathcal{D}_{k}(A)=\bigsqcup_{k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)
we directly obtain (
4.10
).

•

In the case
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)\neq\emptyset
, we use the fact that
(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}\in I_{A}(t)
if and only if
t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}\in[0,t]
and
(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}\in I_{A}\left(t,t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}\right)
(where
I_{A}\left(t,t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}\right)
is defined in the proposition) to rewrite the time integral as

\displaystyle\int_{I_{A}(t)}\hbox{\hskip 18.74896pt\hskip-18.74896pt\hbox{$v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}$}\hskip-18.74896pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.74896pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)},\kappa(\bullet))\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 18.74896pt\hskip-18.74896pt\hbox{$v^{c(\bullet),\varphi(\bullet)}$}\hskip-18.74896pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 18.74896pt}(t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)},\kappa(\bullet))\left(\int_{I_{A}\left(t,t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}\right)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)\mathrm{d}t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}.

Changing variables from
t_{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)}
to
t_{\bullet}
and using
\mathcal{D}_{k}(A)=\bigsqcup_{k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)
we obtain (
4.11
).

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

In the estimates that we will provide it will be useful to discriminate the linear trees according to their number of leaves that are not
\bullet
. To that purpose we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m,n}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{A^{\mathrm{lin}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m}~\middle|~\#\mathcal{L}(A^{\mathrm{lin}})=n\right\}.

Since the number of leaves that are not
\bullet
corresponding to each linear node ranges from
1
(in the case of a linear standard binary node with
\perp
attached) to
2(N(L)+1)
(in the case of a linear ternary node with two elements of
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{N(L)}
attached) we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{n=m}^{2m(N(L)+1)}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m,n}.

As Proposition
4.3
suggests, we distinguish linear trees with respect to the presence or absence of ternary linear nodes and thus define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{(A,\varphi,c)\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m,\eta,\iota}~\middle|~A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m,n},\,\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)=\emptyset,\,\varphi(\bullet)=\iota_{\bullet},\,c(\bullet)=\eta_{\bullet}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{(A,\varphi,c)\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m,\eta,\iota}~\middle|~A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{m,n},\,\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{T}(A)\neq\emptyset,\,\varphi(\bullet)=\iota_{\bullet},\,c(\bullet)=\eta_{\bullet}\right\},

for
n\in\llbracket m,2m(N(L)+1)\rrbracket
. By combining the results of Propositions
4.2
and
4.3
we obtain the following kernel representation in Fourier space of the iterates of
\mathfrak{L}
.

Corollary 4.1
.

If
t\in[0,\delta]
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 15.72505pt\hskip-15.72505pt\hbox{$\mathfrak{L}^{m}(v)^{\eta}$}\hskip-15.72505pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 15.72505pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\\
k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n=m}^{2m(N(L)+1)}\Bigg(\frac{1}{L^{\frac{d}{2}}}\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

(4.12)

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 122.34692pt+\frac{1}{L^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})\mathrm{d}t_{\bullet}\Bigg),

where

\displaystyle Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})}G_{(A,\varphi,c)},

(4.13)

\displaystyle Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})}G_{(A,\varphi,c)}.

(4.14)

Therefore, we have reduced estimating the operator norm of the iterates of
\mathfrak{L}
to estimating
Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
and
Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
. This will be done using coupling of linear trees.

4.1.3
Coupling between linear trees

In order to estimate
\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
and
\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
we will express the quantities

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)

as a sum over couplings of linear trees, similarly to Section
2.7
. We first adapt the notions of this section:

•

First, the notion of coupling between trees from Definition
2.13
can be easily extended to linear trees: if
(A_{1},\varphi_{1},c_{1})
and
(A_{2},\varphi_{2},c_{2})
are two signed and coloured linear trees with
A_{1},A_{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m,n}^{\mathrm{lin}}
for common
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
then
C=\big((A_{1},\varphi_{1},c_{1}),(A_{2},\varphi_{2},c_{2}),\sigma\big)
is a coupling if
\sigma
is an involution of
\mathcal{L}(C)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{L}(A_{1})\sqcup\mathcal{L}(A_{2})
and a bijection from
\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}
to
\mathcal{L}(C)_{+}
(defined again by (
2.36
)-(
2.37
)). The definition is identical, except that each linear tree
A
have the extra leaf
\bullet
which we recall is not included in
\mathcal{L}(A)
. Finally, for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
we define

\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\vcentcolon=\Bigg\{\big((A_{1},\varphi_{1},c_{1}),(A_{2},\varphi_{2},c_{2}),\sigma\big)\>\text{coupling}\,\Bigg|\,\begin{aligned} &(A_{1},\varphi_{1},c_{1})\in\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\\
&(A_{2},\varphi_{2},c_{2})\in\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,-,\eta_{\bullet},-\iota_{\bullet})\end{aligned}\Bigg\}.

•

The notion of a decoration of a coupling from Definition
2.14
extends directly to maps defined on
\mathcal{L}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\sqcup\{\bullet_{1},\bullet_{2}\}
(where
\bullet_{i}
is the extra leaf of
A_{i}
) with the novelty that, in addition to the constraint (
2.38
), we ask that
\kappa(\bullet_{1})+\kappa(\bullet_{2})=0
. The set
\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)
for a given coupling
C=\big((A_{1},\varphi_{1},c_{1}),(A_{2},\varphi_{2},c_{2}),\sigma\big)
is then the set of decoration such that
\kappa(r_{1})=k
and
\kappa(\bullet_{1})=k_{\bullet}
where
r_{1}
and
\bullet_{1}
are respectively the root and the extra leaf of
A_{1}
.

The following proposition is the equivalent of Proposition
2.3
in the context of linear trees.

Proposition 4.4
.

For all
\eta,\eta_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}
,
\iota_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}
and
k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})}\hbox{\hskip 7.29424pt\hskip-7.29424pt\hbox{$G_{C}$}\hskip-7.29424pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.29424pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})}\hbox{\hskip 7.29424pt\hskip-7.29424pt\hbox{$G_{C}$}\hskip-7.29424pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.29424pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet}),

where if
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 7.29424pt\hskip-7.29424pt\hbox{$G_{C}$}\hskip-7.29424pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.29424pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n_{T}(A^{\prime})}(-i)^{n_{T}(C)}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}}{L^{(n(C)-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{-1}t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{+}}M^{c(\sigma(\ell)),c(\ell)}(\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

and if
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 7.29424pt\hskip-7.29424pt\hbox{$G_{C}$}\hskip-7.29424pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.29424pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n_{T}(A^{\prime})}(-i)^{n_{T}(C)}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}}{L^{(n(C)-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{-1}t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}(\Omega_{\bullet}+\Omega_{\bullet^{\prime}})t_{\bullet}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{I_{C}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(C)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{+}}M^{c(\sigma(\ell)),c(\ell)}(\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

where in this last expression
\bullet
and
\bullet^{\prime}
are the two extra leaves in the two linear trees
A
and
A^{\prime}
composing the coupling, and where
I_{C}(t,t_{\bullet})=I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})\times I_{A^{\prime}}(t,t_{\bullet})
.

Proof.

The proof follows exactly the same lines as the one of Proposition
2.3
. We first expand the expectancies using (
4.13
)-(
4.14
) and obtain in the case of
Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\\
(A^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,-,\eta_{\bullet},-\iota_{\bullet})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(G_{(A,\varphi,c)}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})G_{(A^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime},c^{\prime})}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,-k,-k_{\bullet})\right),

where we have used
G^{\iota}_{(A,\varphi,c)}=G_{(A,\iota\varphi,c)}
which can be proved directly using the Fourier expression (
4.10
). Using then this expression again and the Wick formula to extract the nontrivial terms in the expectancy leads to the formula in the proposition. The proof is identical for
Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}
.
∎

Proposition
4.4
reduces estimating
Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
and
Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
to estimating each

G_{C}

⋀

, which will be done in a similar way as for
\widehat{F_{C}}
.

4.1.4
Bushes and dual decorations

In order to estimate the
G_{C}
as defined in Proposition
4.4
, we first need to slightly adapt the notion of bushes introduced in Section
2.8
to coupling of linear trees:

•

The notions of marked and unmarked children from Definition
2.15
is extended to
j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C)\sqcup\{\bullet,\bullet^{\prime}\}
where
\bullet
and
\bullet^{\prime}
are the two extra leaves in the coupling
C
, and
\mathtt{offspring}(j)
is now defined for
j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
by the same formula (
2.46
).

•

The notion of prebush from Definition
2.16
is extended to
j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C)\sqcup\{\bullet,\bullet^{\prime}\}
by defining
\mathtt{prebush}(j)
for
j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}(C)
by (
2.48
) and
\mathtt{prebush}(\bullet)=\mathtt{prebush}(\bullet^{\prime})=\emptyset
. Note that if
\min\left\{j>\bullet~\middle|~\text{$j$ marked}\right\}
exists, its prebush might be reduced to
\mathtt{prebush}(\bullet)
and thus empty, but in this case the prebush of its other marked sibling is not empty.

•

The notion of bush from Definition
2.17
is directly extended to
j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
and the properties of Proposition
2.4
still holds (with
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
replaced by
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
).

•

Self-coupled bushes and
n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)
are defined as in Definition
2.18
. Moreover, the result of Lemma
2.4
becomes (for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
)

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n,q}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\leq\Lambda^{n+1}(n+2-q)!,

(4.15)

where
\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n,q}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
is the set of coupling in
\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
such that
n_{\mathrm{scb}}=q
. Indeed, to go from Lemma
2.4
to (
4.15
), simply note that if
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n,q}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
is a coupling of two linear trees, then these trees can be seen as regular trees both in
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
.

Finally, we also need to adapt the dual point of view on decorations from Definition
3.1
. For a linear coupling, the definition itself is unchanged but recall that
\{\bullet,\bullet^{\prime}\}
don’t belong to
\mathcal{L}(C)
and this has several consequences. If
\kappa
is a decoration of a coupling, it is now uniquely determined by
\kappa_{|_{\mathcal{L}_{+}(C)\sqcup\{\bullet,\bullet^{\prime}\}}}
. Therefore, if
\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)
, it can be seen as an element of
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathcal{L}(C)_{+}}
(since
\kappa(\bullet)
and
\kappa(\bullet^{\prime})
are fixed) and if
j\in\mathcal{L}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)
then

K(j)(\kappa)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\kappa(j)\qquad\qquad\text{if $j\notin\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}(C)$,}\\
&\kappa(j)-k_{\bullet}\qquad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A)$,}\\
&\kappa(j)+k_{\bullet}\qquad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A^{\prime})$,}\end{aligned}\right.

where
A
and
A^{\prime}
are the two linear trees in the coupling. In particular since the root of a linear tree is always a linear tree, we have
K(r)(\kappa)=k-k_{\bullet}
and
K(r^{\prime})(\kappa)=-k+k_{\bullet}
where
r
and
r^{\prime}
are the roots of
A
and
A^{\prime}
.

4.1.5
Low nodes regime for the linear response

In this section, we prove the following proposition, which is the equivalent for
G_{C}
of Proposition
3.2
.

Proposition 4.5
.

There exists
\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\Lambda>0
such that if
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
is a coupling satisfying

\displaystyle n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)+2(n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)}{5},

\displaystyle n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)

\displaystyle\geq 40,

then for any
\varepsilon\leq\delta
and any
k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we have for all
t\in[0,\delta]

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{n[-2]}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}(n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\text{if $k\neq k_{\bullet}$,}\\
&\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d}\delta^{n[-2]}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{low}}(n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{if $k=k_{\bullet}$,}\end{aligned}\right.

where the content of the brackets only appears when
\star=\mathrm{ter}
, in which case the estimate holds for all
0\leq t_{\bullet}\leq t\leq\delta
.

Remark 4.1
.

Compared to the estimate (
3.2
) for
\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)
, we see in the above proposition an extra factor
L^{d}
when
k=k_{\bullet}
. The analogue situation for
\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)
is
k=0
for which the convergence is obvious thanks to the assumption (
1.2
) and its consequence
\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,0)=0
. Note that this extra factor in the estimate for
\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})
is merely polynomial with a fixed degree, still allowing to conclude the fixed point argument, see Section
4.2
.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
4.5
follows the same line as the one of Proposition
3.2
which was spread over all the subsections of Section
3.1
. Therefore, we don’t give as much details here but rather highlight the differences.

First, the result of Lemma
3.1
is slightly changed. Taking into account that
q_{\mathtt{parent}(\bullet)}\lesssim\left\langle\kappa(\bullet)\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and the fact that by definition if
\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)
we have
\kappa(\bullet)=k_{\bullet}
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{-(n-1)d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}\chi_{j}

where
n_{T}^{\bullet}\vcentcolon=\#\left(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}\right)
. Now, if
j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)
and if
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
are its marked children we have

\displaystyle\kappa(j_{1})+\kappa(j_{2})=\sum_{\ell\in\mathtt{offsprings}(j)}\kappa(\ell)+\nu(j)k_{\bullet},

where

\nu(j)=\left\{\begin{aligned} 0&\qquad\text{if $j\notin\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)$,}\\
0&\qquad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)$ but $\bullet$ or $\bullet^{\prime}$ are comparable to the unmarked child of $j$,}\\
1&\qquad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)$ and $\bullet$ is comparable to one of the marked children of $j$,}\\
-1&\qquad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)$ and $\bullet^{\prime}$ is comparable to one of the marked children of $j$.}\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{-(n-1)d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}\chi\left(\left|\sum_{\ell\in\mathtt{offsprings}(j)}\kappa(\ell)+\nu(j)k_{\bullet}\right|\varepsilon^{-\gamma}\right).

The constructions of Section
3.1.1
apply directly to the present context of linear trees, the only difference being due to the terms
\nu(j)k_{\bullet}
in the above formula. The result of Lemma
3.4
is thus changed into the following: there exists a family
(J_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}
of low nodes and functions
\Xi_{J_{i}}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{-(n-1)d}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}\chi\left(\left|\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(J_{i})(\kappa)+\Xi_{J_{i}}\left(\left(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(J_{i^{\prime}})(\kappa)\right)_{i^{\prime}\in\llbracket 1,i-1\rrbracket},k_{\bullet}\right)\right|\varepsilon^{-\gamma}\right),

where
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(j)
is still defined by (
3.12
) and
n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}\geq\frac{n_{\mathrm{low}}-n_{\mathrm{scb}}}{2}
. Since the
J_{i}
are such that
\left(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(J_{i})\right)_{1\leq i\leq n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}
is linearly independent, the change of variables from Section
3.1.2
can still be performed here and we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{-(n-1)d}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(x_{1},\dots,x_{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}\right)\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\right)^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}\\
\left(y_{1},\dots,y_{p}\right)\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{p}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}\chi\left(\left|x_{i}+\Xi_{J_{i}}\left(\left(x_{i^{\prime}}\right)_{i^{\prime}\in\llbracket 1,i-1\rrbracket},k_{\bullet}\right)\right|\varepsilon^{-\gamma}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{i=1}^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}a_{i}x_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}b_{i}y_{i}=k-k_{\bullet}},

where
p=n-n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}
and the only difference with (
3.21
) coming from the fact that the condition
\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)
now translates to
K(r)(\kappa)=k-k_{\bullet}
.

We now estimate the RHS of the above inequality, as we did in Section
3.1.3
. If one of the
b_{i}
is not zero, then the estimates can be performed in the same way: the sum over the
y_{i}
gives a factor
L^{(p-1)d}
and the sum over the
x_{i}
gives a factor
(L^{d}\varepsilon^{\gamma d})^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}
. Using
p=n-n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}
we thus obtain

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}\gamma d+n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

Similarly if one of the
a_{i}
is not zero the sums over the
y_{i}
and the
x_{i}
give a factor
L^{pd}
and
(L^{d}\varepsilon^{\gamma d})^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}-1}
respectively so that we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{(n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}-1)\gamma d+n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

If all the
a_{i}
and the
b_{i}
vanish, then the sum over the
y_{i}
gives a factor
L^{pd}
and the sum over the
x_{i}
gives a factor
(L^{d}\varepsilon^{\gamma d})^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}}
so that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{n_{T}^{\bullet}}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{d}\varepsilon^{n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{low}}\gamma d+n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\mathbbm{1}_{k=k_{\bullet}}.

We can then conclude the proof of the main estimate in Proposition
4.5
from these three estimates as we did at the end of Section
3.1.3
, noting that if
\star=\mathrm{bi}
then
n_{T}^{\bullet}=n_{T}
and if
\star=\mathrm{ter}
then
n_{T}^{\bullet}=n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-2
.
∎

4.1.6
High nodes regime for the linear response

In this section, we prove the following proposition, which is the equivalent for
G_{C}
of Proposition
3.3
.

Proposition 4.6
.

There exists
\alpha_{\mathrm{high}},\Lambda>0
such that if
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
is a coupling satisfying

\displaystyle n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)+2(n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))

\displaystyle<\frac{n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)}{5},

\displaystyle n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)

\displaystyle\geq 30,

\displaystyle n(C)

\displaystyle\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3},

then for any
\varepsilon\leq\delta
and any
k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we have for all
t\in[0,\delta]

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{n[-2]}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}(n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\text{if $k\neq k_{\bullet}$,}\\
&\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d}\delta^{n[-2]}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}(n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{if $k=k_{\bullet}$,}\end{aligned}\right.

where the content of the brackets only appears when
\star=\mathrm{ter}
, in which case the estimate holds for all
0\leq t_{\bullet}\leq t\leq\delta
.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
4.6
follows the same line as the one of Proposition
3.3
which was spread over all the subsections of Section
3.2
. Therefore, we don’t give as much details here but rather highlight the differences. However, as opposed to the low node regime, here the cases
\star=\mathrm{bi}
and
\star=\mathrm{ter}
are different and we start by treating them differently before concluding the estimates for the two cases together.

The case
\star=\mathrm{bi}
.

This case requires almost zero modification since it corresponds to the situation where there are no linear ternary nodes and thus no oscillating integral coming from linear nodes. In particular, the results of Section
3.2.1
apply without any difference and we have

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(C))}\widehat{G_{C}^{\rho}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

where
\widehat{G^{\rho}_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})
satisfies for all
t\in[0,\delta]

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G^{\rho}_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{C}^{\rho}(k,k_{\bullet},\xi)^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i\xi\right|}\mathrm{d}\xi

for some
p>2d
and where

\displaystyle A_{C}^{\rho}(k,k_{\bullet},\xi)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=L^{-(n-1)d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in(\mathcal{N}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C))\setminus\mathcal{R}(C)}\left\langle\kappa(j)\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}\frac{\chi_{j}^{p}}{\left|n_{T}\delta^{-1}-i(\xi-\omega^{\rho}_{j})\right|^{p}},

where
\omega^{\rho}_{j}
is defined as in (
3.33
). From a given
\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}(C))
we can again construct a total order relation
<_{\tilde{\rho}}
on
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C)
as in Section
3.2.2
, the only difference being that
\bullet
and
\bullet^{\prime}
are not included in
\mathcal{L}(C)
. For this reason, the family
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
from Proposition
3.4
is now of cardinal
n
.

The case
\star=\mathrm{ter}
.

This case requires a different treatment of oscillating integrals due to mainly to the difference between
I_{C}(t)
and
I_{C}(t,t_{\bullet})
, which we recall is defined to be
I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})\times I_{A^{\prime}}(t,t_{\bullet})
(where
A
and
A^{\prime}
are the two trees composing the coupling
C
). Therefore we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{C}(t,t_{\bullet})}

\displaystyle\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(C)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

(4.16)

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\times\int_{I_{A^{\prime}}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A^{\prime})\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

Note that there exists a negligible set
\mathcal{Z}_{A}
such that

\displaystyle I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})=\mathcal{Z}_{A}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))}I^{\rho}_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})

(4.17)

where
I^{\rho}_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})
is defined to be the set of
(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}\in[0,t]^{n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1}
such that

\displaystyle 0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))-1)}\leq t_{\bullet}\leq t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}})}\leq t.

By denoting

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\llbracket\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1,n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}\rrbracket\right),\qquad n_{T}^{>_{\rho}}=\#\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\llbracket 1,\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))-1\rrbracket\right),\qquad n_{T}^{<_{\rho}}=\#\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A),

and

\displaystyle I_{A}^{\rho,>}(t,t_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\left\{(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}\in[t_{\bullet},t]^{n_{T}^{>_{\rho}}}~\middle|~t_{\bullet}\leq t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}})}\leq t\right\},

\displaystyle I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\left\{(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}\in[0,t_{\bullet}]^{n_{T}^{<_{\rho}}}~\middle|~0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))-1)}\leq t_{\bullet}\right\},

we find that
I^{\rho}_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})=I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})\times I_{A}^{\rho,>}(t,t_{\bullet})
. We also note that

\displaystyle I_{A}^{\rho,>}(t,t_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(t_{\bullet},\dots,t_{\bullet})+I_{A}^{\rho,>}(t-t_{\bullet},0).

Therefore we have obtained

\displaystyle\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{\bullet}}\int_{I_{A}^{\rho,>}(t-t_{\bullet},0)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\times\int_{I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}.

We can now apply the proof of Lemma
3.5
to these two integrals and get

\displaystyle\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))}e^{i\omega_{\bullet}^{\rho}t_{\bullet}}\frac{e^{\left(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)-1\right)\delta^{-1}t}}{2\pi}

(4.18)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-i\xi(t-t_{\bullet})}}{n_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i\xi}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}\frac{1}{n_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i(\xi-\omega_{j}^{\rho})}\;\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-i\xi t_{\bullet}}}{n_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i\xi}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}\frac{1}{n_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i(\xi-\omega_{j}^{\rho})}\;\mathrm{d}\xi,

where we defined

\omega_{\bullet}^{\rho}\vcentcolon=\varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}\Omega_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\omega_{j}^{\rho}\vcentcolon=\left\{\begin{aligned} \varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)\\
j^{\prime}\geq_{\rho}j\end{subarray}}\Omega_{j^{\prime}},&\quad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)$,}\\
\varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\\
j^{\prime}\geq_{\rho}j\end{subarray}}\Omega_{j^{\prime}},&\quad\text{if $j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)$,}\end{aligned}\right.

and where by convention in the RHS of (
4.18
) if one of the sets
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)
or
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)
is empty then we remove entirely the corresponding integral over
\mathbb{R}
. Of course, a formula similar to (
4.18
) also holds for
A^{\prime}
. Inserting them into (
4.16
) and applying the proof of Lemma
3.6
we finally obtain the following decomposition of
\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})
in the case
\star=\mathrm{ter}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{G_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\\
\rho^{\prime}\in\end{subarray}}\widehat{G_{C}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

where
\widehat{G_{C}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})
satisfies for all
0\leq t_{\bullet}\leq t\leq\delta
:

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{G_{C}^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{A^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}_{C}(k,k_{\bullet},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}\mathrm{d}\xi_{3}\mathrm{d}\xi_{4}}{\left|n_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i\xi_{1}\right|\left|n_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i\xi_{2}\right|\left|n_{T}^{>_{\rho^{\prime}}}(A^{\prime})\delta^{-1}-i\xi_{3}\right|\left|n_{T}^{<_{\rho^{\prime}}}(A^{\prime})\delta^{-1}-i\xi_{4}\right|}

for some
p>2d
and where

\displaystyle A^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}_{C}

\displaystyle(k,k_{\bullet},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=L^{-(n-1)d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in(\mathcal{N}(C)\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C))\setminus\mathcal{R}(C)}\left\langle\kappa(j)\right\rangle^{-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}\frac{\chi_{j}^{p}}{\left|n_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i(\xi_{1}-\omega_{j}^{\rho})\right|^{p}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}\frac{\chi_{j}^{p}}{\left|n_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)\delta^{-1}-i(\xi_{2}-\omega_{j}^{\rho})\right|^{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)}\frac{\chi_{j}^{p}}{\left|n_{T}^{>_{\rho^{\prime}}}(A^{\prime})\delta^{-1}-i(\xi_{3}-\omega_{j}^{\rho^{\prime}})\right|^{p}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}\frac{\chi_{j}^{p}}{\left|n_{T}^{<_{\rho^{\prime}}}(A^{\prime})\delta^{-1}-i(\xi_{4}-\omega_{j}^{\rho^{\prime}})\right|^{p}}.

Again, these formulas hold with the convention that if one of the sets
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho}}(A)
,
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)
,
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho^{\prime}}}(A^{\prime})
or
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{>_{\rho^{\prime}}}(A^{\prime})
is empty then we remove entirely the corresponding integral over
\mathbb{R}
, and in such case
A^{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}_{C}
would only depend on three (or less)
\xi
’s.

The content of Section
3.2.2
must be changed. We have first to define an order given two orders
\rho
,
\rho^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)
,
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\prime})
. Since the nodes in
A
are not comparable to the nodes in
A^{\prime}
, we build
\tilde{\rho}
from the order
\rho*\rho^{\prime}
which is defined by

j^{\prime}<_{\rho*\rho^{\prime}}j\;\iff\;\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cc}j^{\prime}<_{\rho}j&\mathrm{if}\,j,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\\
j^{\prime}<_{\rho^{\prime}}j&\mathrm{if}\,j,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\prime})\\
j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A^{\prime}),j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)&\mathrm{otherwise.}\end{array}}\right.

We define
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)
as before and as in the case
\star=\mathrm{bi}
, it has size
n
.

The common endgame.

The end of the proof holds for both
\star=\mathrm{bi}
and
\star=\mathrm{ter}
. We define the family
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
by

\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)=\Bigg\{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\;\Bigg|\;\begin{aligned} &\mathtt{parent}(j)\notin\{\mathtt{int}(\bullet),\mathtt{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}\\
&j=\min_{\tilde{\rho}}\left(\mathtt{siblings}(j)\cap\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\right)\end{aligned}\;\Bigg\}

while the definition of the sets
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is the same as in Definition
3.6
. The results of Lemma
3.9
must be changed to the following:

(i)

If
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=0
, then
K(\mathtt{root}(A^{\prime}))=0
. In that case, if
k\neq k_{\bullet}
, then
\hbox{\hskip 7.29424pt\hskip-7.29424pt\hbox{$G_{C}$}\hskip-7.29424pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.29424pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})=0
and the estimate of the proposition holds. Otherwise we have
\mathtt{root}(A)\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
and
\mathtt{root}(A^{\prime})\notin\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
.

(ii)

The
\mathcal{P}_{i}
’s satisfy
\mathcal{P}_{3}=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}\subset\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{\mathtt{int}(\bullet),\mathtt{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}
and
\#\mathcal{P}_{1}+2\#\mathcal{P}_{2}\geq n-3
.

(iii)

The cardinal
n^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
satisfy
n^{\prime\prime}\geq\frac{n-3}{2}
.

Moreover, there again exists at least one ternary node that is a parent of a node in
\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)
, otherwise we would have
n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\geq\frac{n-1}{4}
which would contradict
n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)<\frac{n}{5}
and
n\geq 30
according to the high nodes regime assumption. In the case
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\neq 0
, the results in Section
3.2.4
remain valid. But in the case
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=0
and
k=k_{\bullet}
we have no choice but to sum on
n
variables and we obtain

\displaystyle A_{C}^{\rho}(k,k_{\bullet},\xi,[\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}])

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n+1}L^{-(n-1)d}\left(\frac{\delta}{n_{T}}\right)^{p\left(n_{T}^{\bullet}-n^{\prime\prime}_{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{(k_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}\in\left(\frac{1}{L}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\right)^{n-n^{\prime\prime}}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}(C)\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime\prime}(C)}\left\langle k_{j}\right\rangle^{-p}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\left(\sum_{x_{n^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{n^{\prime\prime}}}\left(\cdots\left(\sum_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\tilde{A}_{j_{1}}\right)\cdots\right)\right).

The rest of the proof follows the same lines as what is done in Section
3.2.6
. Regarding the numerology, we now remark that

2n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}\geq n-3-4n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}-8-n_{\mathrm{low}}^{\mathrm{tot}}

from which we deduce

2n^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{gain}}+2n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}\geq\frac{3}{5}(n-n_{\mathrm{scb}})-11

which is bigger that
\frac{n-n_{\mathrm{scb}}}{5}
if
n-n_{\mathrm{scb}}\geq 30
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.6
.
∎

4.1.7
Time derivative of
Y^{\star}_{m,n}

As we did for the
X_{n}
in Section
3.3
, we need to estimate the derivative in time of the
Y^{\star}_{m,n}
for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
. Recall that if
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{lin}}(A)=\emptyset
, then
\mathrm{int}(\bullet)
is not defined and thus in (
4.19
) below the set
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}
simply denotes
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)
.

Proposition 4.7
.

We have

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 15.8305pt\hskip-15.8305pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-15.8305pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 15.8305pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\sum_{(A,\varphi,c)\in\mathcal{A}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})}\hbox{\hskip 21.4088pt\hskip-21.4088pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}G_{(A,\varphi,c)}$}\hskip-21.4088pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.4088pt}

together with the decomposition

\partial_{t}G_{(A,\varphi,c)}

⋀

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\{-1\}\sqcup(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\})}\hbox{\hskip 26.46533pt\hskip-26.46533pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.46533pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.46533pt}

(4.19)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\vcentcolon=\left\{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)~\middle|~\nexists\,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A),\;j^{\prime}>j\right\}

and where

G_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}

⋀

is defined by:

(i)

If
\star=\mathrm{bi}
, then

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.46611pt\hskip-21.46611pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c),-1}$}\hskip-21.46611pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.46611pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-(-i)^{n_{T}+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}}{L^{(n-1)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)}\Omega_{-1}e^{i\Omega_{-1}\varepsilon^{-2}t}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)
, then

G_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}

⋀

\displaystyle(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(-i)^{n_{T}}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+2}}{L^{(n-1)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)}e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}_{A}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell))

where
I^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}_{A}(t)
is defined as in (
3.77
).

(ii)

If
\star=\mathrm{ter}
, then

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.46611pt\hskip-21.46611pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c),-1}$}\hskip-21.46611pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.46611pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\vcentcolon=-(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}}{L^{(n-1)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)}\Omega_{-1}e^{i\Omega_{-1}\varepsilon^{-2}t}e^{i\Omega_{\bullet}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 42.67912pt\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 26.46533pt\hskip-26.46533pt\hbox{$G_{(A,\varphi,c),j_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.46533pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.46533pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\vcentcolon=(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+2}}{L^{(n-1)\frac{d}{2}}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(A)}e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}e^{i\Omega_{\bullet}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 28.45274pt\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)}q_{j}\int_{I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\hbox{\hskip 19.9307pt\hskip-19.9307pt\hbox{$X^{c(\ell),\varphi(\ell)}_{\rm in}$}\hskip-19.9307pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.87904pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.9307pt}(\kappa(\ell)),

where

I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})\vcentcolon=\Bigg\{(t_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}\in[0,t]^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}-2}\;\Bigg|\;j<j^{\prime}\Rightarrow t_{j}<t_{j^{\prime}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;\begin{aligned} &j>\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\Rightarrow t_{j}>t_{\bullet}\\
&j<\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\Rightarrow t_{j}<t_{\bullet}\end{aligned}\Bigg\}.

Proof.

If
\star=\mathrm{bi}
we can repeat the proof of Proposition
3.5
now applied to (
4.10
). If
\star=\mathrm{ter}
, we need to compute the derivative with respect to
t
of the integral over
I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})
. Let
f_{j}
be some functions of time, thanks to (
4.17
) and
I^{\rho}_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})=I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})\times I_{A}^{\rho,>}(t,t_{\bullet})
we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}

\displaystyle\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))\\
\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))\leq n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1\end{subarray}}\int_{I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\int_{t_{\bullet}\leq t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}})}\leq t}\prod_{i=\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}^{n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}}f_{\rho(i)}(t_{\rho(i)})\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))\\
\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))=n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}\end{subarray}}\int_{0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1)}\leq t_{\bullet}}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1}f_{\rho(i)}(t_{\rho(i)})\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}.

In this formula we note that the second sum does not depend on
t
but only on
t_{\bullet}
, while in the first sum only the second integral depends on
t
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))\\
\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))\leq n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1\end{subarray}}\int_{I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\partial_{t}\left(\int_{t_{\bullet}\leq t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}})}\leq t}\prod_{i=\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}^{n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}}f_{\rho(i)}(t_{\rho(i)})\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}\right).

Rewriting the second integral as successive time integrals as we did in the proof of Proposition
3.5
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))\\
\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))\leq n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1\end{subarray}}\int_{I_{A}^{\rho,<}(t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{<_{\rho}}(A)}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times f_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}})}(t)\int_{t_{\bullet}\leq t_{\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1)}\leq t}\prod_{i=\rho(\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))+1)}^{n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1}f_{\rho(i)}(t_{\rho(i)})\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}.

Now, by definition of
\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)
the following map is a bijection

\displaystyle\left\{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A))~\middle|~\rho^{-1}(\mathrm{int}(\bullet))\leq n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1\right\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigsqcup_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}\Big(\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}\times\mathfrak{M}\left(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\rho\hskip 85.35826pt

\displaystyle\longmapsto\hskip 85.35826pt\left(\rho(n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}),\rho_{|_{\left\llbracket 1,n_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}-1\right\rrbracket}}\right)

which allows to perform a change of variable in the last expression and obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\int_{I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}f_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}\left(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}\right)}\int_{I_{A}^{\rho}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\}}f_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t)\int_{I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}f_{j}(t_{j})\mathrm{d}t_{j}.

where we used (
4.17
) now applied to the set
\mathfrak{M}\left(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(A)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}\right)
. We can then use this formula with
f_{j}(t_{j})=e^{i\Omega_{j}t_{j}}
to differentiate (
4.11
) and conclude the proof of the proposition, after the usual dilation in time.
∎

From this proposition and the usual Wick formula, we obtain the following decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 15.8305pt\hskip-15.8305pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-15.8305pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 15.8305pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}],k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)

(4.20)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\mathrm{max}}\in\{-1\}\sqcup(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\})\\
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\in\{-1\}\sqcup(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A^{\prime})\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\})\end{subarray}}\hbox{\hskip 26.02188pt\hskip-26.02188pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.02188pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.02188pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}],k,k_{\bullet})

where
\hbox{\hskip 26.02188pt\hskip-26.02188pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.02188pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.02188pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}],k,k_{\bullet})
is defined by

(i)

If
\star=\mathrm{bi}
, then,

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.02344pt\hskip-16.02344pt\hbox{$G_{C,-1,-1}$}\hskip-16.02344pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.02344pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=-(-i)^{n_{T}}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A)\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})e^{i\Omega_{-1}(C)\varepsilon^{-2}t}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.02266pt\hskip-21.02266pt\hbox{$G_{C,-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-21.02266pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.02266pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=-(-i)^{n_{T}+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+2}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A)e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(A^{\prime})\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}\int_{I^{-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.02266pt\hskip-21.02266pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}$}\hskip-21.02266pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.02266pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=-(-i)^{n_{T}+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+2}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(A)\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 26.02188pt\hskip-26.02188pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.02188pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.02188pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(-i)^{n_{T}}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+4}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(A)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(A^{\prime})\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{I^{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}_{C}(t)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)\setminus\{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

(ii)

If
\star=\mathrm{ter}
, then,

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 16.02344pt\hskip-16.02344pt\hbox{$G_{C,-1,-1}$}\hskip-16.02344pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 16.02344pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=-(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A)\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})e^{i\Omega_{-1}(C)\varepsilon^{-2}t}e^{i\left(\Omega_{\bullet}+\Omega_{\bullet^{\prime}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{I_{C}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.02266pt\hskip-21.02266pt\hbox{$G_{C,-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-21.02266pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.02266pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=-(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+2}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A)e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(A^{\prime})\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}e^{i\left(\Omega_{\bullet}+\Omega_{\bullet^{\prime}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}\int_{I_{C}^{-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(C)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime}),j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

where
I_{C}^{-1,j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})=I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})\times I_{A^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})
,

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 21.02266pt\hskip-21.02266pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}$}\hskip-21.02266pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.02266pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=-(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+2}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}\Omega_{-1}(A^{\prime})e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(A)\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}e^{i\left(\Omega_{\bullet}+\Omega_{\bullet^{\prime}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}\int_{I_{C}^{j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}(C)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime}),j_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

where
I_{C}^{j_{\mathrm{max}},-1}(t,t_{\bullet})=I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})\times I_{A}(t,t_{\bullet})
,

–

if
j_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
and
j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\neq-1
, then

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 26.02188pt\hskip-26.02188pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.02188pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.02188pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(-i)^{n^{\mathrm{tot}}_{T}}\frac{\varepsilon^{n_{B}^{\mathrm{tot}}+4}}{L^{(n-1)d}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k,k_{\bullet}}(C)}e^{i\left(\Omega_{-1}(C)+\Omega_{j_{\mathrm{max}}}+\Omega_{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t}e^{i\left(\Omega_{\bullet}+\Omega_{\bullet^{\prime}}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)}q_{j}\int_{I_{C}^{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot}}(C)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet),\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime}),j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}\}}e^{i\Omega_{j}\varepsilon^{-2}t_{j}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{-}}M^{c(\ell),c(\sigma(\ell))}(\varphi(\ell)\kappa(\ell))^{\varphi(\ell)},

where
I_{C}^{j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})=I_{A}^{j_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})\times I_{A^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}(t,t_{\bullet})
.

Remark 4.2
.

Since the cardinal of
N_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)
grows linearly in
n(A)
, the cardinal of the set

\displaystyle\left\{(C,j_{1},j_{2})~\middle|~C\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n,q}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}),\;j_{i}\in\{-1\}\sqcup(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A_{i})\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet_{i})\}),i=1,2\right\}

where
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are the two trees in
C
, is bounded by
\Lambda^{n+1}\left(n+2-q\right)!
for some
\Lambda>0
.

The following proposition contains the key estimate satisfied by

G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}

⋀

. It is proved as Propositions
4.5
and
4.6
(similarly to how Proposition
3.6
was proved as Proposition
3.1
).

Proposition 4.8
.

Set
\alpha_{0}=\min(\alpha_{\mathrm{low}},\alpha_{\mathrm{high}})
. There exists
\Lambda>0
such that for all
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
satisfying

\displaystyle n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C)\geq 40,\qquad n(C)\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3},

and for all

\displaystyle(j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}})\in\Big(\{-1\}\sqcup(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A)\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet)\})\Big)\times\Big(\{-1\}\sqcup(\mathcal{N}_{T}^{\mathrm{tot},\mathrm{max}}(A^{\prime})\setminus\{\mathrm{int}(\bullet^{\prime})\})\Big),

(where
A
and
A^{\prime}
are the two trees in
C
) then for any
\varepsilon\leq\delta
and any
k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we have for all
t\in[0,\delta]

\displaystyle\left|\hbox{\hskip 26.02188pt\hskip-26.02188pt\hbox{$G_{C,j_{\mathrm{max}},j^{\prime}_{\mathrm{max}}}$}\hskip-26.02188pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 26.02188pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Lambda^{n+1}\delta^{n[-2]}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}(n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\text{if $k\neq k_{\bullet}$,}\\
&\Lambda^{n+1}L^{d}\delta^{n[-2]}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\mathrm{high}}(n(C)-n_{\mathrm{scb}}(C))}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{if $k=k_{\bullet}$,}\end{aligned}\right.

where the content of the brackets only appears when
\star=\mathrm{ter}
, in which case the estimate holds for all
0\leq t_{\bullet}\leq t\leq\delta
.

4.1.8
Estimates for
Y^{\star}_{m,n}
and the iterates

The previous results imply the following proposition.

Proposition 4.9
.

There exists
\Lambda>0
such that, for any fixed
A>0
, with probability
\geq 1-L^{-A}
and for all
n\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,k,k_{\bullet}}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L})^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{d}{2}+A},

\displaystyle\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{t_{\bullet}}L^{\infty}_{k,k_{\bullet}}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]^{2}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L})^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{d}{2}+\frac{2}{\beta}+A}.

Proof.

The
L^{p}
norms in this proof are always taken with respect to the variables
t
and/or
t_{\bullet}
on the interval
[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
. We will omit to write such intervals to keep notations lighter.
Moreover, the estimates that follows are independent of the choice of
\eta
,
\iota
,
\eta_{\bullet}
and
\iota_{\bullet}
, hence we will refer to
Y^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})
simply as
Y^{\star}_{m,n}
for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
.

We begin by observing that, using Proposition
4.4
, (
4.15
), Propositions
4.5
and
4.6
and an argument analogous to the one detailed in the proof of Corollary
3.1
, we can prove the existence of some
\Lambda>0
such that for
\star=\mathrm{bi},\mathrm{ter}
and for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t,t_{\bullet}\in[0,\delta]
we have the estimates

\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.88599pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} \langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-1/2}\Lambda^{2n+1}\delta^{n[-2]}&\qquad\mathrm{if}\,k\neq k_{\bullet},\\
\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-1/2}L^{d}\Lambda^{2n+1}\delta^{n[-2]}&\qquad\mathrm{if}\,k=k_{\bullet}.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.21)

Furthermore, given the definition of
G_{C}
in Proposition
4.4
, we remark that the expectation in the above left hand side is supported for
k,k_{\bullet}
such that
k-k_{\bullet}
belongs to a ball of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L}
centered at the origin, of radius
\mathcal{O}(n)
.

Next, recall that Gaussian hypercontractivity (see for instance Theorem 5.1 from
[
Jan97
]
) gives the existence, for any
p<\infty
, of a constant
c(p)>0
only depending on
p
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.88599pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{p}\right)\leq c(p)^{np}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.88599pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{2}\right)\right)^{p/2}.

Together with (
4.21
) this implies

\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.88599pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,[\varepsilon^{-2}t_{\bullet},]k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{p}\right)\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} c(p)^{np}\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-\frac{p}{4}}\Lambda^{(2n+1)\frac{p}{2}}\delta^{(n[-2])\frac{p}{2}}&\qquad\mathrm{if}\,k\neq k_{\bullet},\\
c(p)^{np}\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-\frac{p}{4}}L^{\frac{dp}{2}}\Lambda^{(2n+1)\frac{p}{2}}\delta^{(n[-2])\frac{p}{2}}&\qquad\mathrm{if}\,k=k_{\bullet}.\end{aligned}\right.

The above estimate being uniform in
t
and
t_{\bullet}
we can integrate it in
t
(and in
t_{\bullet}
if
\star=\mathrm{ter}
) and obtain

\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}_{t}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}_{t,t_{\bullet}}}\right)\\
\leq\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cc}c(p)^{np}\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-\frac{p}{4}}\Lambda^{(2n+1)\frac{p}{2}}\delta^{(n[-1])\frac{p}{2}+1}\varepsilon^{-2[-2]},&\mathrm{if}\,k\neq k_{\bullet},\\
c(p)^{np}\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-\frac{p}{4}}L^{\frac{dp}{2}}\Lambda^{(2n+1)\frac{p}{2}}\delta^{(n[-1])\frac{p}{2}+1}\varepsilon^{-2[-2]},&\mathrm{if}\,k=k_{\bullet}.\end{array}}\right.

(4.22)

Looking at the decomposition (
4.20
) and Proposition
4.8
, we see that we can treat

\partial_{t}Y^{\star}_{m,n}

⋀

identically and thus obtain

\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 21.6929pt\hskip-21.6929pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{N(L),n}$}\hskip-21.6929pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.6929pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}_{t}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 21.6929pt\hskip-21.6929pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{N(L),n}$}\hskip-21.6929pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 21.6929pt}(\cdot,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}_{t,t_{\bullet}}}\right)\\
\leq\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cc}c(p)^{np}\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-\frac{p}{4}}\Lambda^{(2n+1)\frac{p}{2}}\delta^{(n[-1])\frac{p}{2}+1}\varepsilon^{-2[-2]},&\mathrm{if}\,k\neq k_{\bullet},\\
c(p)^{np}\langle k_{\bullet}\rangle^{-\frac{p}{4}}L^{\frac{dp}{2}}\Lambda^{(2n+1)\frac{p}{2}}\delta^{(n[-1])\frac{p}{2}+1}\varepsilon^{-2[-2]},&\mathrm{if}\,k=k_{\bullet}.\end{array}}\right.

(4.23)

Now, using successively the
\ell^{1}\hookrightarrow\ell^{\infty}
injection, the Gagliardo-Niremberg inequality on
[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
(which yields an extra factor
(\varepsilon^{-2}\delta)^{-1}
) and Hölder inequality we deduce the following estimate for

Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}

⋀

:
