\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 15.8305pt\hskip-15.8305pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-15.8305pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 15.8305pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)}+(\varepsilon^{-2}\delta)^{-1}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 15.8305pt\hskip-15.8305pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-15.8305pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 15.8305pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+(\varepsilon^{-2}\delta)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\right)\Bigg).

For

Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}

⋀

, we first use the
L^{p}\hookrightarrow L^{1}
injection on
[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
and then follow the same strategy as for

Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}

⋀

. This gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{1}_{t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\varepsilon^{-2}\delta)^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}_{t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\Bigg((\varepsilon^{-2}\delta)^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 15.8305pt\hskip-15.8305pt\hbox{$\partial_{t}Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-15.8305pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 15.8305pt}(\cdot,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}_{t,t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}_{t,t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+(\varepsilon^{-2}\delta)^{p-2}\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\cdot,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{p}_{t,t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\right)\Bigg).

To estimate the expectations in the above estimates we use (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) with
p>4d
to ensure summability in
k_{\bullet}
, while summability follows from the fact that the expectations in (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) are supported for
k-k_{\bullet}
in a ball of radius
\mathcal{O}(n)
. Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{p}\right)\leq(nL)^{d}L^{\frac{dp}{2}}\Lambda^{\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)p}\delta^{\frac{np}{2}}

(4.24)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{1}_{t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\right)\leq(nL)^{d}L^{\frac{dp}{2}}\Lambda^{\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)p}\delta^{\frac{(n+1)p}{2}-1}\varepsilon^{-2p}.

(4.25)

Setting

\displaystyle a_{n,\bullet}^{\mathrm{bi}}\vcentcolon=\Lambda^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}L^{\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{2}+A},\quad a_{n,\bullet}^{\mathrm{ter}}\vcentcolon=\Lambda^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{n+1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}L^{\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{2}+\frac{2}{\beta}+A},

the estimates (
4.24
)-(
4.25
), together with Markov’s inequality and the assumptions
n\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
and
\varepsilon=L^{-\frac{1}{\beta}}
, finally imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,k,k_{\bullet})\right|^{p}\geq(a_{n,\bullet}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]}\sup_{k,k_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(t,\cdot,k,k_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{1}_{t_{\bullet}}}^{p}\geq(a_{n,\bullet}^{\mathrm{ter}})^{p}\right)\leq L^{-A}.

The conclusion of the proof follows using the fact that we have chosen
p>4d
and from the fact that
\delta<1
.
∎

From this proposition and the representation formula (
4.12
) we deduce the following.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
s\geq 0
and
A>0
. There exists
\Lambda>0
,
\delta_{0}>0
(depending on
d
and
A
) and a set
\mathcal{E}_{L,A}
of probability larger than
1-L^{-A}
such that if
0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}
and when restricted to
\mathcal{E}_{L,A}
then we have for all
m\leq N(L)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{L}^{m}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\sqrt{\delta})^{\frac{m}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{4}+d+A}(\ln L)^{s+d}.

(4.26)

Proof.

Let
t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
be fixed. Using for brevity the notation
I_{m}=\llbracket m,2m(N(L)+1)\rrbracket
, the representation formula (
4.12
) and the Fourier version of
Y^{\star,\iota^{\prime}}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})=Y^{\star}_{m,n}(\eta,\iota^{\prime}\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}\iota_{\bullet})
we obtain the decomposition

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{L}^{m}(v)^{\eta}(t,\cdot)\right\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle=S_{\mathrm{bi}}+S_{\mathrm{cross}}+S_{\mathrm{ter}},

where

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{bi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet},\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\\
k,k_{\bullet},k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\\
n,n^{\prime}\in I_{m}\\
\end{subarray}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2s}\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(t,k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 13.05261pt\hskip-13.05261pt\hbox{$v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-13.05261pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{20.42464pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.05261pt}(t,-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(t,k,k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 12.768pt\hskip-12.768pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n^{\prime}}$}\hskip-12.768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.768pt}(\eta,-,\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet})(t,-k,-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}),

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{ter}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{4}}{L^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet},\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\\
k,k_{\bullet},k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\\
n,n^{\prime}\in I_{m}\\
\end{subarray}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2s}\int_{[0,t]^{2}}\Bigg(\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(t_{\bullet},k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(t,t_{\bullet},k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\times\hbox{\hskip 13.05261pt\hskip-13.05261pt\hbox{$v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-13.05261pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{20.42464pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.05261pt}(t^{\prime}_{\bullet},-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,-,\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet})(t,t^{\prime}_{\bullet},-k,-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}t_{\bullet}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}_{\bullet},

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{cross}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{L^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet},\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota,\iota_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\\
k,k_{\bullet},k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\\
n,n^{\prime}\in I_{m}\\
\end{subarray}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2s}\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(t,k_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 12.768pt\hskip-12.768pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n^{\prime}}$}\hskip-12.768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.768pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(t,k,k_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\int_{0}^{t}\hbox{\hskip 13.05261pt\hskip-13.05261pt\hbox{$v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-13.05261pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{20.42464pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.05261pt}(t_{\bullet},-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\hbox{\hskip 12.768pt\hskip-12.768pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n^{\prime}}$}\hskip-12.768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{18.9346pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.768pt}(\eta,-\iota,\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet})(t,t_{\bullet},-k,-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\mathrm{d}t_{\bullet}.

In order to estimate
S_{\mathrm{bi}}
,
S_{\mathrm{ter}}
and
S_{\mathrm{cross}}
, we will use the following simple key facts:

•

Thanks to the definition of
N(L)
(see the very beginning of Section
4
) and the link between
L
and
\varepsilon
(that is
L=\varepsilon^{-\beta}
), if
m\leq N(L)
then we have
2m(N(L)+1)\leq 3\beta^{2}|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}
which is less than
|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
if
L
is large enough.

•

For any fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
,
\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\star}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.88599pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,\iota,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(t,[t_{\bullet},]k,\cdot)
is supported in a ball centered at
k
and of radius
\mathcal{O}(n)
.

We proceed with estimating
S_{\mathrm{bi}}
. For any fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
we use the change of variables
k_{\bullet}\longmapsto k_{\bullet}-k
and
k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\longmapsto k^{\prime}_{\bullet}-k
and bound
\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2s}
by
\left\langle k-k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}
to obtain the bound

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{bi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet},\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in I_{m}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k_{\bullet}|\lesssim n\\
|k^{\prime}_{\bullet}|\lesssim n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left\langle k-k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(t,k_{\bullet}-k)\right|\left|\hbox{\hskip 13.05261pt\hskip-13.05261pt\hbox{$v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-13.05261pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{20.42464pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.05261pt}(t,k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\times\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})(t,k,k_{\bullet}-k)\right|\left|\hbox{\hskip 12.768pt\hskip-12.768pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n^{\prime}}$}\hskip-12.768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.768pt}(\eta,-,\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet})(t,-k,k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet},\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in I_{m}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle n^{\prime}\right\rangle^{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,k,k_{\bullet}}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L})^{2})}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 12.768pt\hskip-12.768pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n^{\prime}}$}\hskip-12.768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.768pt}(\eta,-,\eta_{\bullet},-\iota_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,k,k_{\bullet}}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L})^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k_{\bullet}|\lesssim n\\
|k^{\prime}_{\bullet}|\lesssim n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left\langle k-k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(t,k_{\bullet}-k)\right|\left|\hbox{\hskip 13.05261pt\hskip-13.05261pt\hbox{$v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-13.05261pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{20.42464pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.05261pt}(t,k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\right|

where we bound
\left\langle k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}
by
\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle n^{\prime}\right\rangle^{s}
and estimated the two

Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}

⋀

and

Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n^{\prime}}

⋀

in
L^{\infty}
. Exchanging the sums in the last line and using Cauchy-Schwartz inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k_{\bullet}|\lesssim n\\
|k^{\prime}_{\bullet}|\lesssim n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left\langle k-k_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet}\right\rangle^{s}

\displaystyle\left|\hbox{\hskip 11.70538pt\hskip-11.70538pt\hbox{$v^{\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-11.70538pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.64293pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.70538pt}(t,k_{\bullet}-k)\right|\left|\hbox{\hskip 13.05261pt\hskip-13.05261pt\hbox{$v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}}$}\hskip-13.05261pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{20.42464pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 13.05261pt}(t,k-k^{\prime}_{\bullet})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k_{\bullet}|\lesssim n\\
|k^{\prime}_{\bullet}|\lesssim n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left\|v^{\eta_{\bullet}}(t,\cdot)\right\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\left\|v^{\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(t,\cdot)\right\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{d}(n^{\prime})^{d}L^{2d}\left\|v(t,\cdot)\right\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}.

Therefore, with probability larger than
1-L^{-A}
we have

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{bi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|v(t,\cdot)\right\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}L^{d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{\bullet},\eta^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{0,1\}\\
\iota_{\bullet},\iota^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in I_{L}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\langle n^{\prime}\right\rangle^{s}n^{d}(n^{\prime})^{d}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\left\|\hbox{\hskip 11.41556pt\hskip-11.41556pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n}$}\hskip-11.41556pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 11.41556pt}(\eta,+,\eta_{\bullet},\iota_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,k,k_{\bullet}}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L})^{2})}\left\|\hbox{\hskip 12.768pt\hskip-12.768pt\hbox{$Y^{\mathrm{bi}}_{m,n^{\prime}}$}\hskip-12.768pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 12.768pt}(\eta,-,\eta_{\bullet},-\iota_{\bullet})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,k,k_{\bullet}}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]\times(\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L})^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}\left([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta],H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})\right)}^{2}L^{\frac{1}{2}+2d+2A}\left(\sum_{n\in I_{m}}n^{s+d}\Lambda^{n}\delta^{\frac{n}{2}}\right)^{2}

where we used the result of Proposition
4.9
since in the sums over
I_{m}
we indeed have
n\leq|\ln\varepsilon|^{3}
. Choosing
\delta
small enough such that
\Lambda\sqrt{\delta}<1
and recalling that
I_{m}=\llbracket m,2m(N(L)+1)\rrbracket
and
m\leq N(L)
we obtain

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{bi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}\left([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta],H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})\right)}^{2}L^{\frac{1}{2}+2d+2A}N(L)^{2(s+d)}(\Lambda\sqrt{\delta})^{m}.

The same procedure can be applied to
S_{\mathrm{cross}}
and
S_{\mathrm{ter}}
using also the second estimate in Proposition
4.9
(note that
\varepsilon^{2}
and
\varepsilon^{4}

S_{\mathrm{cross}}
and
S_{\mathrm{ter}}
will be compensated by the
L^{\frac{2}{\beta}}
in the
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{t_{\bullet}}L^{\infty}_{k,k_{\bullet}}
estimate for

Y^{\mathrm{ter}}_{m,n}

⋀

). We have thus proved that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{L}^{m}(v)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}\left([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta],H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})\right)}\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{4}+d+A}(\ln L)^{s+d}(\Lambda\sqrt{\delta})^{\frac{m}{2}}\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}\left([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta],H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})\right)},

which concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

4.2
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Now that we have estimated the operator norm of the iterates of the linear response, we can conclude the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

The estimate (
4.26
) applied with
m=N(L)
shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{L}^{N(L)}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\Lambda L^{\frac{1}{4}\ln\left(\delta\right)+\frac{1}{2}\ln\Lambda+\frac{1}{4}+d+A}(\ln L)^{s+d}.

Therefore, choosing
\delta
small enough depending only on
d
and
A
so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\ln\left(\delta\right)+\frac{1}{2}\ln\Lambda+\frac{1}{4}+d+A<0

we can then choose
L_{0}
large enough depending only on
s
,
d
and
A
so that
\left\|\mathfrak{L}^{N(L)}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}<1
for all
L\geq L_{0}
. Therefore, when restricted to the set
\mathcal{E}_{L,A}
, the operator
\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}^{N(L)}
is invertible and, thanks to the identity (
4.7
), so is
\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}
with

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{N(L)-1}\left\|\mathfrak{L}^{m}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\Lambda L^{\frac{1}{4}+d+A}(\ln L)^{s+d}\sum_{m=0}^{N(L)-1}(\Lambda\sqrt{\delta})^{\frac{m}{2}},

where we again used (
4.26
). Choosing
\delta
small, bounding the sum by a universal constant and bounding
(\ln L)^{s+d}
by
L^{A}
for
L
large enough we have proved that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq L^{d+\frac{1}{4}+2A}.

(4.27)

We now consider the mapping

\displaystyle v\longmapsto\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathfrak{S}+\mathfrak{N}(v)\right)

(4.28)

where
\mathfrak{S}
and
\mathfrak{N}(v)
have been defined in (
4.3
) and (
4.6
) respectively. We prove that for a particular choice of
\Lambda_{0}>0
, the above map sends the set

\displaystyle Z_{s,A,\Lambda_{0}}\vcentcolon=\left\{v\in\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))~\middle|~\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda_{0}L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}\right\}

onto itself. The contraction property follows similarly, we leave the details to the reader.

We first estimate the source term
\mathfrak{S}
. Starting from (
4.4
) and using (
4.2
), we find that for
\eta\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=0}^{N(L)}X_{n}^{\eta}(t)+X_{0}^{\eta}(t)-\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(0,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{0},X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{N(L)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{n_{1}}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{n_{2}}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-i\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}=0}^{N}\sum_{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}}C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{n_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{n_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{n_{3}}(\tau)\right)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Regrouping terms with respect to the value of
n_{1}+n_{2}
or
n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}
and using (
2.4
) and (
2.5
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1},n_{2}\leq N(L)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\geq N(L)\\
\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{n_{1}}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{n_{2}}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-i\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\leq N(L)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\geq N(L)-1\\
\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{n_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{n_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{n_{3}}(\tau)\right)\mathrm{d}\tau.

The bilinear (resp. trilinear) operators in the above right hand side, defined in
(
2.1
) (resp. (
2.2
)), are continuous operators from
\left(\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))\right)^{2}
(resp.
\left(\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))\right)^{3}
) to
\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))
whenever
s>d/2
. Indeed, although there are not multiplication in the physical space, the facts that the functions
Q^{\eta}
are bounded and that the
L^{1}
norm of the Fourier transform is bounded by the
H^{s}
norm, we deduce the boundedness of the operators in a similar way as to prove that
H^{s}
is a Banach algebra. Therefore, from Corollary
3.1
we deduce that there exists some
\Lambda>0
such that for any
t\in[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
, we have that

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1},n_{2}\leq N(L)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\geq N(L)\\
\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\left\|B^{\eta}_{(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})}\left(t,X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{1}}_{n_{1}}(t),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{n_{2}}(t)\right)\right\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{N(L)}{2}}L^{d+\frac{1}{\beta}+2A},

recalling that assuming that
\delta
is small enough ensures the convergence of the series
\sum(\Lambda\delta)^{n/2}
. Similarly, we have that

\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{*}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\leq N(L)\\
n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\geq N(L)-1\\
\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3}\in\{\pm\}\end{subarray}}\|C^{\eta}_{*,(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3})}\left(\tau,X^{\eta,\iota_{1}}_{n_{1}}(\tau),X^{\bar{\eta},\iota_{2}}_{n_{2}}(\tau),X^{\eta,\iota_{3}}_{n_{3}}(\tau)\right)\|_{H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d})}\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\leq(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{3N(L)}{4}+1}L^{3(\frac{d}{2}+\frac{1}{\beta}+A)}.

We deduce the following estimates

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{S}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{N(L)}{2}}L^{3(\frac{d}{2}+\frac{1}{\beta}+A)},

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\mathfrak{S}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(\Lambda\delta)^{\frac{N(L)}{2}}L^{\frac{5}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3}{\beta}+5A},

where we used (
4.27
) for the second one. Therefore, since
N(L)\leq\ln L
, we can choose
\delta
small enough so that

\displaystyle\frac{7}{2}d+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{\beta}+8A+\frac{1}{2}\ln\Lambda+\frac{1}{2}\ln\delta<0

and obtain

\left\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L})^{-1}\mathfrak{S}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}.

As for the nonlinear term defined in (
4.6
), using again Corollary
3.1
and
\varepsilon=L^{-\frac{1}{\beta}}
, we find that for
v\in Z_{s,A,\Lambda_{0}}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{N}(v)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left(1+\varepsilon\left\|X_{\leq N(L)}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\varepsilon\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}\Lambda_{0}^{2}L^{-\left(d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{2}{\beta}+2A\right)}.

Using now (
4.27
) this gives

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}(v))\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\lesssim L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}\Lambda_{0}^{2}L^{-\frac{2}{\beta}}.

We have thus proved that there exists a universal constant
\Lambda>0
such that if
v\in Z_{s,A,\Lambda_{0}}
then

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathfrak{L}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathfrak{S}+\mathfrak{N}(v)\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta];H^{s}(L\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda L^{-\left(\frac{3}{2}d+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{\beta}+3A\right)}\left(1+\Lambda_{0}^{2}L^{-\frac{2}{\beta}}\right).

We then choose
\Lambda_{0}=\frac{\Lambda}{2}
and
L
large enough so that
\Lambda_{0}^{2}L^{-\frac{2}{\beta}}<1
, and the above estimate then shows that the mapping (
4.28
) sends
Z_{s,A,\Lambda_{0}}
to itself. We can prove similarly that it is in fact a contraction.
∎

This also concludes the proof of Theorem
4.1
, after having applied the Banach fixed point theorem to (
4.28
).

5
Resonant nodes and resonant system

In this section, we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.1
by showing that (
1.15
) is well-posed on
[0,\delta]
and approaches well the correlations associated to (
1.12
) over the time interval
[0,\varepsilon^{-2}\delta]
. In particular, we identify the resonant couplings (Sections
5.1
and
5.2
), i.e couplings whose contribution to the correlations survives in the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
. The well-posedness of (
1.15
) is proved in Section
5.3
, while the final comparison argument is performed in Section
5.4
.

5.1
Resonant nodes

We start by giving the definition of resonant nodes.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
C
be a coupling with sign map
\varphi
and let
j\in\mathcal{N}(C)
. We say that
j
is a
resonant node
if the following holds:

(i)

j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
,

(ii)

\mathtt{bush}(j)
is self-coupled, in the sense of Definition
2.18
,

(iii)

we have

\displaystyle\varphi(j_{1})=-\varphi(j_{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi(j_{3})=\varphi(j),

where
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
are the marked children of
j
and
j_{3}
is its unmarked child.

The goal of this section is to obtain appropriate estimates for
F_{C}
defined in (
2.39
)
according to whether or not all nodes in
C
are resonant. The main result is the following.

Proposition 5.1
.

There exist
\alpha,\Lambda>0
such that, for all couplings
C
with at least one non-resonant node, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{\frac{n(C)}{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha},

for all
\varepsilon\leq\delta
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}
.

The proof of the above proposition is detailed in in Section
5.1.2
and it is based on intermediate lemmas contained in Section
5.1.1
.

5.1.1
Properties of resonant nodes

We gather here some properties of resonant nodes.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
C
be a coupling and
j\in\mathcal{N}(C)
be such that

\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C),\quad j^{\prime}\leq j\Longrightarrow\left\{\begin{aligned} &j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(C),\\
&\text{$\mathtt{bush}(j^{\prime})$ is self-coupled.}\end{aligned}\right.

(5.1)

(i)

If
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
are the marked children of
j
, then
K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})=0
and
K(j)=K(\ell)
for some
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)
with
\ell\leq j
.

(ii)

Moreover, if
j_{3}
is the unmarked child of
j
, then
(K(j_{1}),K(j_{3}))
is linearly independent.

Proof.

We prove statement
(i)
by induction on the height
h
of
j
, which is the maximal distance between
j
and a leaf
\ell\leq j
in the graph representing the coupling.

•

If
h=1
, i.e if the children of
j
are leaves, then
\mathtt{bush}(j)=\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}
where
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
are its two marked children. By assumption
\mathtt{bush}(j)
is self-coupled so
\sigma(\ell_{1})=\ell_{2}
(where
\sigma
denotes the coupling map of
C
) and
K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{2})=0
(recall Definition
3.1
). Since
K(j)=K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{2})+K(\ell_{3})
(where
\ell_{3}
is the unmarked children of
j
) we also obtain
K(j)=K(\ell_{3})
.

•

Assume now
h>1
and that
(i)
holds for all nodes satisfying (
5.1
) and of height strictly less than
h
.
We write
\mathtt{children}(j)=\{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}\}
with
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
its marked children. Recalling (
2.46
), (
2.55
) and (
3.12
) we have

\displaystyle K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})=\sum_{\ell\in\mathtt{offsprings}(j)}K(\ell)=\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(j)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)\\
j^{\prime}\leq j_{1}\text{ or }j^{\prime}\leq j_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(j^{\prime}).

By assumption on
j
, we have
\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}(j^{\prime})=0
for all
j^{\prime}\leq j
so that
K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})=0
. This implies
K(j)=K(j_{3})
and since
j_{3}
satisfies (
5.1
) and its height is strictly less than
h
, by induction there must exist
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C)
such that
\ell\leq j_{3}
, and thus
\ell\leq j
, satisfying
K(\ell)=K(j_{3})=K(j)
.

In order to prove
(ii)
, we first observe that the children of
j
satisfy (
5.1
). From
(i)
we thus derive the existence of three distinct leaves
\ell_{i}
such that
K(j_{i})=K(\ell_{i})
for
i=1,2,3
. Since
K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{2})=K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})=0
, we deduce that
\ell_{1}=\sigma(\ell_{2})
and consequently
\sigma(\ell_{3})\notin\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}
, which indeed shows that
K(\ell_{3})
and
K(\ell_{1})
, and thus
K(j_{3})
and
K(j_{1})
, are linearly independent.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
C
be a coupling and
j\in\mathcal{N}(C)
be such that

\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C),\quad j^{\prime}\leq j\Longrightarrow\text{$j^{\prime}$ is resonant.}

(5.2)

Then, for any
j^{\prime}\leq j
and any decoration map of
C
, we have
\Delta_{j^{\prime}}=\Omega_{j^{\prime}}=0
and

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}=c_{j}t^{n_{j}},

where we use the notations

\displaystyle n_{j}

\displaystyle=\#\left\{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C)~\middle|~j^{\prime}\leq j\right\},

\displaystyle c_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{j}!}\#\mathfrak{M}\big(\left\{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C)~\middle|~j^{\prime}\leq j\right\}\big),

\displaystyle I_{j}(t)

\displaystyle=\left\{(t_{j^{\prime}})_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C),j^{\prime}\leq j}\in[0,t]^{n_{j}}~\middle|~j^{\prime}_{1}<j^{\prime}_{2}\Longrightarrow t_{j^{\prime}_{1}}<t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}\right\}.

Proof.

Assume
j
satisfies (
5.2
) and
j^{\prime}\leq j
. In particular,
j^{\prime}
admits no binary nodes below it and
\Delta_{j^{\prime}}=\Omega_{j^{\prime}}
. Moreover,
j^{\prime}
satisfies (
5.1
) and from Lemma
5.1
we deduce that
K(j^{\prime}_{1})+K(j^{\prime}_{2})=0
and
K(j^{\prime})=K(j^{\prime}_{3})
, where
j^{\prime}_{1}
and
j^{\prime}_{2}
are the two marked children of
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime}_{3}
its unmarked child. Therefore, using the relation
K(j)(\kappa)=\kappa(j)
and the sign properties of the resonant node
j^{\prime}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\Delta_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\varphi(j^{\prime})\left\langle\kappa(j^{\prime})\right\rangle-\varphi(j^{\prime}_{3})\left\langle\kappa(j^{\prime}_{3})\right\rangle-\varphi(j^{\prime}_{1})\left\langle\kappa(j^{\prime}_{1})\right\rangle-\varphi(j^{\prime}_{2})\left\langle\kappa(j^{\prime}_{2})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\varphi(j^{\prime})-\varphi(j^{\prime}_{3})\right)\left\langle\kappa(j^{\prime})\right\rangle-\left(\varphi(j^{\prime}_{1})+\varphi(j^{\prime}_{2})\right)\left\langle\kappa(j^{\prime}_{1})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0.

To evaluate the integral, we use the analogue of (
3.32
) for
I_{j}(t)
and write

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}_{j}}\int_{0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{j})}\leq t}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{j}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{\rho(i)}t_{\rho(i)}}\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}

where
\mathfrak{M}_{j}
denotes
\mathfrak{M}\big(\left\{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C)~\middle|~j^{\prime}\leq j\right\}\big)
. Since all
\Omega_{\rho(i)}=0
, we conclude

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}_{j}}\int_{0\leq t_{\rho(1)}<\cdots<t_{\rho(n_{j})}\leq t}\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(i)}\cdots\mathrm{d}t_{\rho(n_{j})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in\mathfrak{M}_{j}}\frac{t^{n_{j}}}{n_{j}!}

\displaystyle=c_{j}t^{n_{j}}.

∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
C
be a coupling and
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
be such that

\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(C),\quad j^{\prime}<j\Longrightarrow\text{$j^{\prime}$ is resonant.}

(5.3)

For all decoration maps of
C
and for all
t\in[0,\delta]
we have

\left|\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} \delta^{n_{j}}&\quad\text{if $|\Omega_{j}|\leq 3\varepsilon^{2}\delta^{-1}$,}\\
3\delta^{n_{j}-1}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|\Omega_{j}|}&\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

By hypothesis, all children
{j_{i}}
,
i=1,2,3
, of
j

satisfy (
5.2
) so the previous lemma implies that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j_{i}}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq{j_{i}}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}=c_{j_{i}}t^{n_{j_{i}}}

and

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1,2,3}\int_{I_{j_{i}}(t_{j})}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq{j_{i}}}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right)\mathrm{d}t_{j}

\displaystyle=c_{j_{1}}c_{j_{2}}c_{j_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}t_{j}^{n_{j_{1}}+n_{j_{2}}+n_{j_{3}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j}.

Since
n_{j_{1}}+n_{j_{2}}+n_{j_{3}}=n_{j}-1
,

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=c_{j_{1}}c_{j_{2}}c_{j_{3}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}\tau}\tau^{n_{j}-1}\mathrm{d}\tau

Using
c_{i}\leq 1
and
n_{j}\geq 1
, we obtain the following bound independently of the value of
\Omega_{j}

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{j}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\leq j}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|\leq\delta^{n_{j}}.

In addition, if
\Omega_{j}\neq 0
we can perform an integration by parts

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}\tau}\tau^{n_{j}-1}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t}-1}{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}}t^{n_{j}-1}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}t}}{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}}(n_{j}-1)\tau^{n_{j}-2}\mathrm{d}\tau,

then simply bound the imaginary exponentials by 1 in order to obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}\tau}\tau^{n_{j}-1}\mathrm{d}\tau\right|\leq 3\frac{\delta^{n_{j}-1}}{\varepsilon^{-2}|\Omega_{j}|},

and the conclusion of the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
C
be a coupling such that
n_{B}(C)=0
and such that there exists
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
satisfying (
5.3
). For all decoration maps of
C
and all
t\in[0,\delta]
, we have

\left|\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} \delta^{n_{T}(C)}&\quad\text{if $|\Omega_{j}|\leq 3\varepsilon^{2}\delta^{-1}$,}\\
3\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|\Omega_{j}|}&\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

(5.4)

Proof.

Let
A
and
A^{\prime}
denote the two trees in
C
and assume, without loss of generality, that
j
satisfying (
5.3
) is a ternary node in
A
. By definition of
I_{C}(t)
we have

\displaystyle\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\times\int_{I_{A^{\prime}}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A^{\prime})}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}.

We bound the latter integral in the above right hand side simply by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{A^{\prime}}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A^{\prime})}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|\leq\delta^{n_{T}(A^{\prime})}.

(5.5)

In order to conclude the proof, it is therefore enough to prove the following statement by induction on
n\geq 2
: for all
n\geq 2
and for all signed coloured trees
A
of size
n
with
n_{B}(A)=0
and such that there exists
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)
satisfying (
5.3
), we have the bound (
5.4
) with
C
replaced by
A
.

•

If
n=2
then
j
is the root,
I_{j}(t)=I_{A}(t)
and the result of Lemma
5.3
applies.

•

Assume the induction hypothesis is true up to some
n\geq 2
and let
A
be a signed coloured tree in
\tilde{A}_{n+2}
with
n_{B}(A)=0
and with some
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)
satisfying (
5.3
). Then
A=T_{*}(A_{1},A_{2},A_{3})
for some
A_{i}\in\tilde{A}_{n_{i}}
with
n_{i}\leq n
and
n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}=n
. If
j=r_{A}
coincides with the root of
A
, the result of Lemma
5.3
applies again. If
j\neq r_{A}
, we can assume without loss of generality that
j\in A_{1}
, then use the induction hypothesis on
A_{1}
and (
5.5
) to the integrals over
I_{A_{2}},I_{A_{3}}
to derive

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{A}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{r}\tau}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\int_{I_{A_{i}}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A_{i})}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{1+n_{T}(A_{2})+n_{T}(A_{3})}\left|\int_{I_{A_{1}}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A_{1})}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{1+n_{T}(A_{2})+n_{T}(A_{3})}\delta^{n_{T}(A_{1})}\min\left(1,\frac{3\varepsilon^{2}}{\delta|\Omega_{j}|}\right).

Since
n_{T}(A)=1+n_{T}(A_{1})+n_{T}(A_{2})+n_{T}(A_{3})
, this concludes the proof.

∎

5.1.2
Proof of Proposition
5.1

We will make use of the following simple lemma.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
C=(A,A^{\prime},\sigma)
be a coupling and
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{L}(A)
. If
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\in\mathrm{Span}\left\{K(\ell)~\middle|~\ell\in\mathcal{F}\right\}
then

\displaystyle K(\mathtt{root}(A))=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{F},\sigma(\ell)\notin\mathcal{L}(A)}K(\ell)

(5.6)

and moreover
\#\left\{\ell\in\mathcal{F}~\middle|~\sigma(\ell)\notin\mathcal{L}(A)\right\}
has the same parity of
\mathcal{L}(A)
.

Proof.

By assumption, there exist numbers
s_{\ell}
such that

\displaystyle K(\mathtt{root}(A))=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{F}}s_{\ell}K(\ell)

(5.7)

Since
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}K(\ell)
, (
5.7
) rewrites
\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)}\mu_{\ell}K(\ell)=0
where
\mu_{\ell}=1
if
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)\setminus\mathcal{F}
and
\mu_{\ell}=1-s_{\ell}
if
\ell\in\mathcal{F}
. Using
K(\ell)=-K(\sigma(\ell))
, we can reformulate this equality as a relation involving only sums over positive distinct leaves

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)_{+}\\
\ell\notin\sigma(\mathcal{L}(A)_{-})\end{subarray}}\mu_{\ell}K(\ell)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)_{+}\\
\ell\in\sigma(\mathcal{L}(A)_{-})\end{subarray}}(\mu_{\ell}-\mu_{\sigma(\ell)})K(\ell)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\notin\mathcal{L}(A)_{+}\\
\ell\in\sigma(\mathcal{L}(A)_{-})\end{subarray}}\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}K(\ell)

\displaystyle=0.

But the maps
\{K(\ell)\,|\,\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)_{+}\}
are linearly independent, therefore all coefficients in the above left hand side are zero. Defining
\mathfrak{C}(A)\vcentcolon=\left\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)~\middle|~\sigma(\ell)\in\mathcal{L}(A)\right\}
, this implies that for all
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(A)
we have

\displaystyle\ell\in\mathfrak{C}(A)\Longrightarrow\mu_{\ell}=\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}\quad\text{and}\quad\ell\notin\mathfrak{C}(A)\Longrightarrow\mu_{\ell}=0.

(5.8)

Now let
\ell\in\mathcal{F}
, so that
\mu_{\ell}=1-s_{\ell}
. We distinguish three cases:

•

If
\ell\in\mathfrak{C}(A)
and
\sigma(\ell)\in\mathcal{F}
, then
\mu_{\ell}=\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}
and
\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}=1-s_{\sigma(\ell)}
which imply
s_{\ell}=s_{\sigma(\ell)}
. Therefore, in this case we can remove
\ell
and
\sigma(\ell)
from the identity (
5.7
).

•

If
\ell\in\mathfrak{C}(A)
and
\sigma(\ell)\notin\mathcal{F}
, then
\mu_{\ell}=\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}
and
\mu_{\sigma(\ell)}=1
which imply
s_{\ell}=0
. Also in this case,we can remove
\ell
from (
5.7
).

•

If
\ell\notin\mathfrak{C}(A)
, then
\mu_{\ell}=0
and so
s_{\ell}=1
.

We have thus proved (
5.6
). Another consequence of the definition of
\mu_{\ell}
and (
5.8
) is that
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathfrak{C}(A)=\mathcal{L}(A)
. This implies that

\displaystyle\#\left\{\ell\in\mathcal{F}~\middle|~\sigma(\ell)\notin\mathcal{L}(A)\right\}=\#\mathcal{L}(A)-\#\mathfrak{C}(A),

which concludes the proof of the lemma since
\#\mathfrak{C}(A)
is even.
∎

We are now in the position to prove Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Let us first assume that
C
has at least one binary node, i.e
n_{B}(C)\geq 1
. Then, when estimating the right hand side of (
2.40
), we simply bound the time integral by
\delta^{n_{T}(C)}
, the
q_{j}
s by 1 and the
M^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}
by some universal constant, then make the standard change of variable in the sum over
\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)
using factor
L^{-\frac{n(C)d}{2}}
and obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\hbox{\hskip 7.27272pt\hskip-7.27272pt\hbox{$F_{C}$}\hskip-7.27272pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.27272pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)}\varepsilon^{n_{B}(C)}.

Since
n(C)=n_{B}(C)+2n_{T}(C)
,
\varepsilon\leq\delta
and
n_{B}(C)\geq 1
, this gives

\displaystyle\left|\hbox{\hskip 7.27272pt\hskip-7.27272pt\hbox{$F_{C}$}\hskip-7.27272pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 7.27272pt}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{\frac{n(C)}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Assume now that
n_{B}(C)=0
and that
C
admits at least one non-resonant ternary node
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
j
satisfies (
5.3
) and belongs to
A
. Therefore, Lemma
5.4
applies and (
5.4
) holds. Applying the inequality
\min\left(1,\frac{a}{b}\right)\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}a}{\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}}
with
a=3\delta^{-1}
and
b=\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)}e^{i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j^{\prime}}t_{j^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}t_{j^{\prime}}\right|\leq\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}\frac{3\sqrt{2}}{\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}\right|}.

Plugging this into (
2.40
), using the compact support assumption for the
M^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}
and bounding all the
q_{j^{\prime}}
for
j^{\prime}\neq j
by 1, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\frac{\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}}{L^{n_{T}(C)d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\frac{|q_{j}|}{\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}\right|},

(5.9)

where we also used that
n(C)=2n_{T}(C)
. In order to estimate the RHS of this inequality we distinguish between the nature of
j
, i.e. if it is a low node or a high node.

Case 1:
j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(C)
.

We denote by
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
its marked children and by
j_{3}
its unmarked child, and we further distinguish between two situations.

Case 1.1:
\mathtt{bush}(j)
is self-coupled.

In this case,
j
satisfies satisfies (
5.1
) and the first part of Lemma
5.1
implies

\displaystyle\Omega_{j}

\displaystyle=(\varphi(j)-\varphi(j_{3}))\left\langle\kappa(j_{3})\right\rangle-(\varphi(j_{1})+\varphi(j_{2}))\left\langle\kappa(j_{1})\right\rangle.

Since
j
is not resonant, then the quantities
\varphi(j)-\varphi(j_{3})
and
\varphi(j_{1})+\varphi(j_{2})
(which take value in
\{-2,0,2\}
) cannot vanish simultaneously. If they differ from each other, then we have
|\Omega_{j}|\gtrsim\left\langle\kappa(j_{i})\right\rangle\gtrsim m
for some
i=1,3
so that (
5.9
) becomes

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}\varepsilon^{2},

which also concludes the proof since
\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}\varepsilon^{2}\leq\delta^{n_{T}(C)}\varepsilon=\delta^{\frac{n(C)}{2}}\varepsilon
. Therefore the only remaining case is when they are both equal, so that
\Omega_{j}=2\iota\left(\left\langle\kappa(j_{3})\right\rangle-\left\langle\kappa(j_{1})\right\rangle\right)
for some
\iota\in\{\pm\}
. The second part of Lemma
5.1
implies that there exists leaves
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{3}
such that
K(\ell_{i})=K(j_{i})
for
i=1,3
and
K(\ell_{1})
and
K(\ell_{3})
are linearly independent.

•

If
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\notin\mathrm{Span}\{K(\ell_{1}),K(\ell_{3})\}
then we can complete
\left(K(\mathtt{root}(A)),K(\ell_{1}),K(\ell_{3})\right)
into a basis of
V(C)
with its initial basis: there exists leaves
\ell_{m}\in\mathcal{L}(C)_{+}
for
m=4,\dots,n_{T}(C)+1
such that
\left(K(\mathtt{root}(A)),K(\ell_{1}),K(\ell_{3}),K(\ell_{4}),\dots,K(\ell_{n_{T}(C)+1})\right)
is a basis of
V(C)
. By performing a change of variable similar to the one of Section
3.1.2
and applying Lemma
2.2
to
q_{j}
, we deduce from (
5.9
) that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\frac{\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}}{L^{n_{T}(C)d}}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{2}\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}2\iota\left(\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle-\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle\right)\right|}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\sum_{(k_{r},k_{4},\dots,k_{n_{T}(C)+1})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{n_{T}(C)-1}}\mathbbm{1}_{k_{r}=k}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-2d}\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle^{-1}\left(\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)}\frac{1}{\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{2}\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}2\iota\left(\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle-\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle\right)\right|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)d}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle^{-1}\left(\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)}\frac{1}{\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{2p}\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}2\iota\left(\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle-\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle\right)\right|^{p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

where we used Hölder’s inequality for some
p>\frac{d}{2}
. Using now Lemma
3.11
and a similar proof than the one of (
3.67
) in the proof of Lemma
3.13
we can prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)}\frac{1}{\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{2p}\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}2\iota\left(\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle-\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle\right)\right|^{p}}\lesssim L^{d}\left(\varepsilon^{\beta-2}+\delta^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2}\right),

so that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\beta-2}+\delta^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}L^{-d}\sum_{k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\beta-2}+\delta^{p-1}\varepsilon^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}L^{-\frac{d}{q}}

where we again used Hölder’s inequality on the last sum for some
q>d
. By choosing in addition
q<2d
and using
\varepsilon\leq\delta
one can show that this implies

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)}\varepsilon^{\min\left(\frac{\beta-2}{p},\frac{\beta d}{q}\right)}.

(5.10)

•

If now
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\in\mathrm{Span}\{K(\ell_{1}),K(\ell_{3})\}
, then
\#\mathcal{L}(A)=2n_{T}(A)+1
and the result of Lemma
5.5
show that
K(\mathtt{root})=K(\ell_{i})
for some
i\in\{1,3\}
, say
i=3
without loss of generality. We then complete
\left(K(\ell_{1}),K(\ell_{3})\right)
into a basis of
V(C)
as above, and the constraint on the root of
A
translates as a constraint on
K(\ell_{3})
. After the usual change of variable we again obtain from (
5.9
):

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-d}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{2}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{k_{3}=k}}{\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}2\iota\left(\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle-\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle\right)\right|}.

We can then conclude as above and obtain (
5.10
) again.

Case 1.2:
\mathtt{bush}(j)
is not self-coupled.

Since every node below
j
is resonant, we have
K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})=\mathfrak{K}_{\mathrm{bush}}
and thus
K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})
is not identically zero. As above, Lemma
5.1
implies that there exists leaves
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{3}
such that
K(\ell_{i})=K(j_{i})
for
i=1,3
. Moreover, we have that
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\notin\mathrm{Span}\{K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{2})\}
since if
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\in\mathrm{Span}\{K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{2})\}
then Lemma
5.5
would imply
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=K(\ell_{i})
for some
i=1,2
and thus
K(\ell_{i})\in\mathrm{Span}\{K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{2})\}
which is absurd. Therefore we complete
\left(K(j_{1})+K(j_{2}),K(\mathtt{root}(A))\right)
into a basis
\left(K(j_{1})+K(j_{2}),K(\mathtt{root}(A)),K(\ell_{3}),\dots,K(\ell_{n_{T}(C)+1})\right)
of
V(C)
and obtain from (
5.9
):

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\frac{\delta^{n_{T}(C)}}{L^{n_{T}(C)d}}\sum_{\left(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},\dots,k_{n_{T}(C)+1}\right)\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{n_{T}(C)+1}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k_{1}|\leq\varepsilon^{\gamma}}\mathbbm{1}_{k_{2}=k}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)}\varepsilon^{\gamma d}.

Case 2:
j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{high}}(C)
.

We denote again by
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
and
j_{3}
the children of
j
. As above, Lemma
5.1
implies that there exists distinct leaves
\ell_{i}
such that
K(\ell_{i})=K(j_{i})
for
i=1,2,3
. Since the leaves are distinct and satisfy
K(\ell_{i})\neq 0
(by definition of
V(C)
), we can assume that
K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})\neq 0
and
K(j_{1})+K(j_{3})\neq 0
. Note that among the five situations characterizing a high node, either all the interactions are high, or
K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})
or
K(j_{1})+K(j_{3})
are low. Therefore (
5.9
) implies

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)\right|

\displaystyle\leq B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3},

where

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)}L^{-n_{T}(C)d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|K(j_{1})(\kappa)+K(j_{2})(\kappa)|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}},

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)}L^{-n_{T}(C)d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|K(j_{1})(\kappa)+K(j_{3})(\kappa)|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}},

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-n_{T}(C)d}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)\\
\kappa(\mathcal{L}(C)_{+})\subset B(0,R)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{|K(j_{1})(\kappa)+K(j_{2})(\kappa)|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}\mathbbm{1}_{|K(j_{1})(\kappa)+K(j_{3})(\kappa)|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}\mathbbm{1}_{|K(j_{3})(\kappa)+K(j_{2})(\kappa)|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}}{\left\langle K(j_{1})(\kappa)\right\rangle\left\langle K(j_{2})(\kappa)\right\rangle\left\langle K(j_{3})(\kappa)\right\rangle\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}\Omega_{j}\right|}.

For
B_{1}
, we again note that
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\notin\mathrm{Span}\{K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})\}
so that we can complete
\left(K(j_{1})+K(j_{2}),K(\mathtt{root}(A))\right)
into a basis of
V(C)
and do as in Case 1.2. The case of
B_{2}
is identical by considering
K(\ell_{1})+K(\ell_{3})
instead. We thus obtain

\displaystyle B_{1}+B_{2}\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)}\varepsilon^{\gamma d}.

For
B_{3}
, we first note that if
\left(K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3})\right)
, i.e.
\left(K(\ell_{1}),K(\ell_{2}),K(\ell_{3})\right)
is not linearly independent, then there must exists
i_{1}\neq i_{2}\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
\sigma(\ell_{i_{1}})=\ell_{i_{2}}
so that
K(j_{i_{1}})+K(j_{i_{2}})=0
and
B_{3}=0
. We can thus assume that
\left(K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3})\right)
is linearly independent.

•

If
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\notin\mathrm{Span}\{K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3})\}
, then we complete
\left(K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3}),K(\mathtt{root}(A))\right)
into a basis of
V(C)
and ultimately obtain

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-3d}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{3}}\frac{\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle k_{2}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{|k_{3}+k_{2}|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}}{\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\varphi(j)\left\langle k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{1})\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{2})\left\langle k_{2}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{3})\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle\right)\right|}.

By summing first on
k_{1}
and using again Lemmas
3.11
and
3.13
we can conclude as in Case 1.1.

•

If
K(\mathtt{root}(A))\in\mathrm{Span}\{K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3})\}
, then Lemma
5.5
implies that either
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=K(j_{i})
for some
i=1,2,3
or
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})+K(j_{3})
. If say
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=K(j_{1})
we complete
\{K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3})\}
into a basis and translate the constraint on the root to a constraint on
K(j_{1})(\kappa)
to obtain

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-2d}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{(k_{2},k_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{2}}\frac{\left\langle k_{2}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{|k+k_{2}|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}}{\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\varphi(j)\left\langle k+k_{2}+k_{3}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{1})\left\langle k\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{2})\left\langle k_{2}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{3})\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle\right)\right|}.

If
K(\mathtt{root}(A))=K(j_{1})+K(j_{2})+K(j_{3})
we again complete
\{K(j_{1}),K(j_{2}),K(j_{3})\}
into a basis and translate the constraint on the root to a constraint on
K(j_{1})(\kappa)+K(j_{2})(\kappa)+K(j_{3})(\kappa)
and obtain

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{n(C)+1}\delta^{n_{T}(C)-1}L^{-2d}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{(k_{2},k_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{d}\cap B(0,R)\right)^{2}}\frac{\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle k_{2}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle k_{3}\right\rangle^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{|k-k_{1}|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\gamma}}}{\left|3\delta^{-1}+i\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\varphi(j)\left\langle k\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{1})\left\langle k_{1}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{2})\left\langle k_{2}\right\rangle-\varphi(j_{3})\left\langle k-k_{1}-k_{2}\right\rangle\right)\right|}.

In both cases, by summing first on
k_{2}
we can conclude using Lemmas
3.11
and
3.13
as in Case 1.1

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

5.2
Resonant couplings

In this section, we introduce the notion of resonant couplings and ultimately show that their recursive structure is the one of binary trees.

5.2.1
First properties and reduction

In the following remark we introduce a useful way of denoting signed coloured tree.

Remark 5.1
.

Let
(A,\varphi,c)
be a signed coloured tree with
A\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
with
n>0
and assume that
\mathtt{root}(A)
is a
*
-ternary node (resp.
*
-binary node). Then
A
writes
\left(T_{*}\left(\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{A}_{2},\tilde{A}_{3}\right),\varphi,c\right)
(resp.
\left(B_{*}\left(\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{A}_{2}\right),\varphi,c\right)
). We allow ourselves to also write
A
as a labbelled tree, that is to say, we allow the abuse of notation
A=(T_{*}(A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}),\iota,\eta)
(resp.
A=(B_{*}(A_{1},A_{2}),\iota,\eta)
) where for
i=1,2,3
(resp.
i=1,2
), we set
A_{i}=\left(\tilde{A}_{i},\varphi_{|\mathcal{N}(\tilde{A}_{i})\cup\mathcal{L}(\tilde{A}_{i})},c_{|\mathcal{N}(\tilde{A}_{i})\cup\mathcal{L}(\tilde{A}_{i})}\right)
and
\iota=\varphi(\mathtt{root}(A))
,
\eta=c(\mathtt{root}(A))
.

Definition 5.2
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
\mathtt{Res}_{n}
to be the set of couplings with
n
nodes that are all resonant (in the sense of Definition
5.1
). We also define

\displaystyle\mathtt{Res}(\iota,\iota^{\prime},\eta,\eta^{\prime})=\mathtt{Res}_{n}\cap\bigcup_{n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{C}^{\iota,\iota^{\prime},\eta,\eta^{\prime}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}.

Thanks to

\int_{I_{C}(t)}\prod_{j}dt_{j}=\#\mathfrak{M}(C)\frac{t^{n}}{n!},

we can easily prove from (
2.40
) that if
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}
then we have

\displaystyle\widehat{F_{C}}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,k)=(-i)^{n}L^{-nd}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(C)}q_{j}\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{-}(C)}M^{c(l),c(\sigma(l))}(-\kappa(l))\#\mathfrak{M}(C)\frac{t^{n}}{n!}.

(5.11)

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}
with
n>0
. There exists
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
n_{1}+n_{2}=n-1
,
\iota\in\{\pm\}
,
\eta\in\{0,1\}
and
(C_{1},C_{2})\in\mathtt{Res}_{n_{1}}\times\mathtt{Res}_{n_{2}}
such that if we write
C_{i}=(A_{i},A^{\prime}_{i},\sigma_{i})
and define
\sigma:\mathcal{L}(C_{1})\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(C_{1})\sqcup\mathcal{L}(C_{2})
by

\sigma(\ell)=\left\{{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sigma_{1}(\ell)&\mathrm{if}\,\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C_{1}),\\
\sigma_{2}(\ell)&\mathrm{if}\,\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C_{2}),\end{array}}\right.

then we are in one of the following situations

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle((T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}),\iota,\eta),A^{\prime}_{2},\sigma)

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle((T_{\mathrm{low-}m}(A_{1},A_{2},A_{1}^{\prime}),\iota,\eta),A^{\prime}_{2},\sigma)

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle((T_{{\mathrm{low-}\ell}}(A_{2},A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime}),\iota,\eta),A^{\prime}_{2},\sigma)

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle(A_{2},(T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}^{\prime})\iota,\eta),\sigma)

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle(A_{2},(T_{\mathrm{low-}m}(A_{1},A_{2}^{\prime},A_{1}^{\prime})\iota,\eta),\sigma)

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle(A_{2},(T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(A_{2}^{\prime},A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime})\iota,\eta),\sigma).

Moreover, if
\iota=\iota^{\prime}
then
\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,\iota^{\prime},\eta,\eta^{\prime})=\emptyset
.

Remark 5.2
.

We note that the different situations for
C
are not exclusive.

Proof.

Assume
C=(A,A^{\prime},\sigma)
belongs to
\mathtt{Res}_{n}
with
n>0
. Since
n>0
, either
\mathtt{root}(A)
or
\mathtt{root}(A^{\prime})
is a node, and because this node is resonant, it is a low node. We assume that
\mathtt{root}(A)
is a
{\mathrm{low-}r}
node, which takes us to the first situation above (the other possibilities corresponding to the other cases). In this case, we get that
A=(T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}),\iota,\eta)
for some
\iota,\eta
(recall Remark
5.1
). We have already seen that
\mathtt{offsprings}(\mathtt{root}(A))
is self-coupled, which tells us that

C_{1}=\left(A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{|\mathcal{L}(A_{1})\cup\mathcal{L}(A_{1}^{\prime})}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad C_{2}=\left(A_{2},A^{\prime},\sigma_{|\mathcal{L}(A_{2})\cup\mathcal{L}(A^{\prime}_{2})}\right)

are couplings. We remark that the bush of a given node
j
is a subset of the leaves below
j
. Therefore, if
j\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{low}}(A_{1})
, the set
\mathtt{bush}(j)
does not depend on whether we consider it in
A_{1}
or in
A
. What we mean by this is that being resonant, although it is not a local property, depends only on what happens below a given node. Therefore the nodes in
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are resonant and we deduce
C_{i}\in\mathtt{Res}_{n_{i}}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}+1=n
.

We turn to the second part of the lemma and assume
\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,\iota^{\prime},\eta,\eta^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
. We prove by induction on
n
that
\iota=-\iota^{\prime}
. If
n=0
, this is due to the definition of a coupling. If
n>0
, let
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,\iota^{\prime},\eta,\eta^{\prime})
, thanks to the first part of the lemma we are in one of the situations described above. In all cases, due to the sign condition on resonant nodes, we have that
C_{2}\in\mathtt{Res}_{n_{2}}(\iota,\iota^{\prime},\eta_{2},\eta_{2}^{\prime})
. This yields by the induction hypothesis that
\iota^{\prime}=-\iota
.
∎

Definition 5.3
.

Let
C
be a coupling.

(i)

We say that a node is a left-resonant (resp. middle-resonant, resp right-resonant) node as a resonant node whose unmarked child is its left (resp. middle, resp. right) child.

(ii)

We define
\mathcal{R}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})
to be the set of couplings
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})
whose nodes are all right-resonant.

The following proposition shows that we can reduce resonant couplings to resonant couplings with only right-resonant nodes.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})
.

(i)

If
C
has a middle-resonant node, then
F_{C}=0
.

(ii)

If
C
has no middle-resonant node, there exists
\tilde{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})
such that
F_{C}=F_{\tilde{C}}
and if
C=(A,A^{\prime},\sigma)
and
\tilde{C}=(\tilde{A},\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{\sigma})
, then
n_{T}(A)=n_{T}(\tilde{A})
.

(iii)

We have

\sum_{C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})}F_{C}=2^{n}\sum_{C\in\mathcal{R}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})}F_{C}.

Remark 5.3
.

This proposition reduces considerably the amount of resonant nodes that might contribute to the limit. We remark that without the assumption (
1.2
), middle-resonant nodes would come into play. In what follows, we explain how to pass from resonant couplings to binary trees, making emerge the recursive structure of resonant couplings. For this, we use an algorithm that takes into account the type of resonant nodes we consider. With a larger number of types of resonant nodes, we get a larger number of cases in the algorithm.

Proof.

For the first part of the proposition, simply note that (
1.2
) implies that if
k_{1}+k_{3}=0
, then
q_{\iota}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=0
for all
k_{2}
and
\iota
. Therefore, if
C
has a middle-resonant node
j
, then
q_{j}=0
and thus
F_{C}=0
.

For the second part of the proposition, we first note using (
2.40
) that if
C=(A,A^{\prime},\sigma)
is a coupling then

\widehat{F_{C}}(t,k)=\hbox{\hskip 19.23196pt\hskip-19.23196pt\hbox{$F_{(A^{\prime},A,\sigma)}$}\hskip-19.23196pt\hskip-4.16667pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\lx@scalerel@obj{\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt}}}}$}}\hskip-4.16667pt\hskip 19.23196pt}(t,-k).

Also note that we can rewrite
\widehat{F_{C}}(t,k)
with the help of (
5.11
) as

\widehat{F_{C}}(t,k)=\tilde{F}_{C}(k)\#\mathfrak{M}(C)\frac{t^{n}}{n!},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{C}(k)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(-i)^{n}L^{-nd}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{N}(C)}q_{j}\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{-}(C)}M^{c(l),c(\sigma(l))}(-\kappa(l)).

We wish to prove the second part of the proposition on
\tilde{F}_{C}
,
\#\mathfrak{M}(C)\frac{t^{n}}{n!}
and
n_{T}(A)
by induction. If
n=0
and
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{0}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})
then it only has right-resonant nodes. If now
n>0
and
C\in\mathtt{Res}_{n}(\iota,-\iota,\eta,\eta^{\prime})
and
C=(A,A^{\prime},\sigma)
, we are in the situation described in Lemma
5.6
. We describe the first three situations, that is, keeping the notations of the lemma,

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle((T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}),\iota,\eta),A^{\prime}_{2},\sigma),

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle((T_{\mathrm{low-}m}(A_{1},A_{2},A_{1}^{\prime}),\iota,\eta),A^{\prime}_{2},\sigma),

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle((T_{{\mathrm{low-}\ell}}(A_{2},A_{1},A_{1}^{\prime}),\iota,\eta),A^{\prime}_{2},\sigma),

the others we may deduce by symmetry. In these three situations, we have

\tilde{F}_{C}(k)=-iL^{-d}\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L}}q_{\mathtt{root}(A)}\tilde{F}_{C_{1}}(k_{1})\tilde{F}_{C_{2}}(k).

Indeed,
\kappa
belongs to
\mathcal{D}_{k}(C)
if and only if there exists
k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L}
,
\kappa_{1}\in\mathcal{D}_{k_{1}}(C_{1})
and
\kappa_{2}\in\mathcal{D}_{k}(C_{2})
such that for all
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C_{1})
, we have
\kappa(\ell)=\kappa_{1}(\ell)
and for all
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(C_{2})
, we have that
\kappa(\ell)=\kappa_{2}(\ell)
. We treat each of the three situations separately:

•

As for the first part of the proposition, if we are in the second situation then
q_{\mathtt{root}(A)}=0
and thus
F_{C}=0
.

•

We place ourselves in the first situation, that is to say
\mathtt{root}(A)
is right-resonant. If
C_{1}
or
C_{2}
has a middle-resonant node then we have either
\tilde{F}_{C_{1}}=0
or
\tilde{F}_{C_{2}}=0
and thus
F_{C}=0
. Otherwise, the induction assumption implies that there exist couplings
\tilde{C}_{1}
and
\tilde{C}_{2}
with only right-resonant nodes such that
\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{1}}=\tilde{F}_{C_{1}}
and
\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{2}}=\tilde{F}_{C_{2}}
and
\#\mathfrak{M}(\tilde{C}_{1})=\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{1})
and
\#\mathfrak{M}(\tilde{C}_{2})=\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{2})
. We prove that

\#\mathfrak{M}(C)=\frac{n!}{n_{T}(A)n_{1}!n_{2}!}\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{1})\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{2}).

Indeed, we have that

\#\mathfrak{M}(C)=\frac{n!}{n_{T}(A)!n_{T}(A_{2}^{\prime})!}\#\mathfrak{M}(A)\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{2}^{\prime}).

This last equality is due to the fact that an order
\rho
on
\mathcal{N}_{T}(C)
is obtained from an order
\rho_{1}
on
\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)
and an order
\rho_{1}^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{N}_{T}(A^{\prime})
by chosing
n_{T}(A)
integers between
1
and
n
, that is to say a strictly increasing map
\mu
from
\llbracket 1,n_{T}(A)\rrbracket
into
\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
. Setting
\mu^{\prime}
the only strictly increasing map from
\llbracket 1,n_{T}(A^{\prime})\rrbracket
into the complementary set in
\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
of the image of
\mu
, we define
\rho(j)=\mu(\rho_{1}(j))
if
j\in\mathcal{N}_{T}(A)
and
\rho(j)=\mu^{\prime}(\rho_{1}^{\prime}(j))
otherwise. For similar reasons, we have that

\#\mathfrak{M}(A)=\frac{(n_{T}(A)-1)!}{n_{T}(A_{1})!n_{T}(A_{2})!n_{T}(A_{3})!}\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{1})\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{2})\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{3}).

Rearranging the product, we get that

\#\mathfrak{M}(C)=\frac{n!}{n_{T}(A)n_{1}!n_{2}!}\frac{n_{1}!}{n_{T}(A_{1})!n_{T}(A_{1}^{\prime})!}\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{1})\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{1}^{\prime})\frac{n_{2}!}{n_{T}(A_{2})!n_{T}(A_{2}^{\prime})!}\#\mathfrak{M}(A_{2})\#\mathfrak{M}(A^{\prime}_{2}).

We recognize

\#\mathfrak{M}(C)=\frac{n!}{n_{T}(A)n_{1}!n_{2}!}\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{1})\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{2}).

Set
\tilde{A}=(T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{A}_{2}),\iota,\eta)
where
\tilde{C}_{1}=(\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{\sigma}_{1})
and
\tilde{C}_{2}=(\tilde{A}_{2},\tilde{A}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\sigma}_{2})
and
\tilde{C}=(\tilde{A},\tilde{A}_{2},\tilde{\sigma})
where
\tilde{\sigma}
is such that
\tilde{\sigma}(\ell)=\tilde{\sigma}_{1}(\ell)
if
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(\tilde{C}_{1})
and
\tilde{\sigma}(\ell)=\tilde{\sigma}_{2}(l)
otherwise. We have that

\displaystyle n_{T}(\tilde{A})=

\displaystyle 1+n_{T}(\tilde{C}_{1})+n_{T}(\tilde{A}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+n_{T}(C_{1})+n_{T}(A_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n_{T}(A).

Thanks to the induction formula on
\#\mathfrak{M}(C)
, we get
\#\mathfrak{M}(C)=\#\mathfrak{M}(\tilde{C})
. Moreover, we have
q_{\mathtt{root}(A)}=q_{\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})}
since

\displaystyle q_{\mathtt{root}(A)}=

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathrm{low-}r}(k_{1},-k_{1},k)q_{\iota\varphi(\mathtt{root}(A_{1}^{\prime}))}^{\eta}(k_{1},-k_{1},k),

\displaystyle q_{\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})}=

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathrm{low-}r}(k_{1},-k_{1},k)q_{\iota\varphi(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime}))}^{\eta}(k_{1},-k_{1},k).

By the induction assumption, we also have that
\varphi(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime}))=\varphi(\mathtt{root}(A_{1}^{\prime}))
, so that we have finally proved that

\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}}=\tilde{F}_{C}.

It remains to check that
\tilde{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{n}
. First, we check that it is indeed a coupling in the sense that it satisfies the colour condition. We have that
c(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}))=\eta=c(\mathtt{root}(A))
. We deduce that

(c(\mathtt{root}(A_{1})),c(\mathtt{root}(A_{1}^{\prime})),c(\mathtt{root}(A_{2})))=(\eta,\bar{\eta},\eta).

Therefore,
(c(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{1})),c(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime})),c(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{2})))=(\eta,\bar{\eta},\eta)
which yields that
\tilde{C}
is indeed a coupling. We check that
\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})
is a right-resonant node. By definition, it is a
{\mathrm{low-}r}
-ternary node. What is more, its offsprings set is self-coupled, which, knowing that all the bushes below are self-coupled, implies that the bush of
\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})
is self-coupled. The sign of
\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})
is
\iota
, so is the sign of
\mathtt{root}(A)
. We deduce that the sign of
\mathtt{root}(A_{2})
is
\iota
and thus so is the sign of
\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{2})
which makes
\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})
a right-resonant node.

•

Assume now that we are in the third situation that is to say that
\mathtt{root}(A)
is left-resonant. If
C_{1}
or
C_{2}
has a middle-resonant node, then we have either
\tilde{F}_{C_{1}}=0
or
\tilde{F}_{C_{2}}=0
and thus
\tilde{F}_{C}=0
. Otherwise, there exist couplings
\tilde{C}_{1}
and
\tilde{C}_{2}
with only right-resonant nodes such that
\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{1}}=\tilde{F}_{C_{1}}
and
\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{2}}=\tilde{F}_{C_{2}}
and
\#\mathfrak{M}(\tilde{C}_{1})=\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{1})
and
\#\mathfrak{M}(\tilde{C}_{2})=\#\mathfrak{M}(C_{2})
.

Set
\tilde{A}=(T_{\mathrm{low-}r}(\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{A}_{2}),\iota,\eta)
where
\tilde{C}_{1}=(\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{\sigma}_{1})
and
\tilde{C}_{2}=(\tilde{A}_{2},\tilde{A}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\sigma}_{2})
and
\tilde{C}=(\tilde{A},\tilde{A}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\sigma})
where
\tilde{\sigma}
is such that
\tilde{\sigma}(\ell)=\tilde{\sigma}_{1}(\ell)
if
\ell\in\mathcal{L}(\tilde{C}_{1})
and
\tilde{\sigma}(\ell)=\tilde{\sigma}_{2}(\ell)
otherwise. As above we have that
n_{T}(\tilde{A})=n_{T}(A)
. Thanks to the induction formula on
\#\mathfrak{M}(C)
, we also get
\#\mathfrak{M}(C)=\#\mathfrak{M}(\tilde{C})
. We also have that

\tilde{F}_{C}(k)=-iL^{-d}\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L}}q_{\mathtt{root}(A)}\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{1}}(k_{1})\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{2}}(k)

while

\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}}(k)=-iL^{-d}\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{L}}q_{\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})}\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{3}}(k_{1})\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{2}}(k)

where
\tilde{C}_{3}=(\tilde{A}_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{A}_{1},\tilde{\sigma}_{1})
. We have

q_{\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A})}=\iota\chi_{\mathrm{low-}r}(k_{1},-k_{1},k)q_{\iota\varphi(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{1}))}^{\eta}(k_{1},-k_{1},k),

where by the induction hypothesis
\varphi(\mathtt{root}(\tilde{A}_{1}))=\varphi(\mathtt{root}(A_{1}))
. What is more,

q_{\mathtt{root}(A)}=\iota\chi_{{\mathrm{low-}\ell}}(k,k_{1},-k_{1})q_{\iota\varphi(\mathtt{root}(A_{1}))}^{\eta}(k,k_{1},-k_{1}).

Using that
\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{1}}(k_{1})=\tilde{F}_{\tilde{C}_{3}}(-k_{1})
